## F-09 — Agent prompts + L2 review

- **Commits:** `feat/f09-agent-prompts-l2-review` → PR (this one). Red-first in two steps, both captured: (1) the new suite failed at collection on the missing `factory.l2` module before any implementation; (2) with everything implemented *except* the schema change, `test_ac_6` failed red against the pre-F-09 schemas with `Additional properties are not allowed ('standards' was unexpected)` — the AC-6 "red before the schema change" evidence — and only then were the schemas edited.
- **Built:** 2026-10-17.
- **Design notes:**
  - **Prompts are orchestrator-side, always.** The seat prompt *files* live in the repo at `prompts/`, which the sandbox list-denies by design (F-06), so `factory.prompts` reads and assembles them outside the session and the text rides in via `options.system_prompt` — `SeatConfig.system_prompt` stays empty and `build_options`/`run_session`/`run_session_sandboxed` gained a `system_prompt` parameter (the spec file carries it across the sandbox boundary). The staged runner copy inside the sandbox never reads `prompts/` — nothing in the entry path imports `factory.prompts`.
  - **Assembly (VALIDATOR-SPEC L2 "standards live with the reviewer"):** `assemble_reviewer_prompt` = base `prompts/reviewer.md` + every `project.yaml standards` file's content (or an explicit "documents no standards files: the smell baseline applies alone" statement — the report must say so); `assemble_implementer_prompt` = base `prompts/implementer.md` + project mechanics only (test command, `test_ref`, protected paths). A `standards` entry naming a missing file raises `PromptError` — a reviewer silently judging without its standards is worse than a loud refusal.
  - **Prompt versioning:** `prompt_versions(profile)` = seat → sha256 of the *assembled* prompt, so both a base-file edit and an assembly change show up as a new version (the shape F-10's `attempts[].prompt_versions` records, already schema'd in F-02). The base files are pinned by content hash in `tests/test_f09.py::PROMPT_PINS` (the JobFinderOS pattern) — the pin is the only sanctioned way to change a prompt.
  - **L2 (`factory/l2.py`) parses, never trusts:** `parse_verdict` extracts the JSON object (whole text → fenced block → outermost brace span; first parse wins), validates the shape, and computes the verdict. Any violation — no JSON, non-object, missing/unknown `verdict`, non-list findings, non-object finding, axis not exactly `spec`/`standards`, missing text, non-string severity, non-integer line — is *returned* as `verdict=fail` with one `invalid_verdict_json` finding and `parse_error` set (AC-1: treated as FAIL, never an exception — a bad reviewer stops the line, not the orchestrator). A `blocking` finding on **either** axis fails whatever the declared verdict and other axis say (AC-4); declared `fail` stands without blocking findings; non-blocking findings don't force a fail. `L2Report.to_dict()` carries `axis`/`severity` per finding.
  - **L1 `red_reason` (AC-7):** best-effort text classification of each red run — `collection` (file-level `ERROR` short-summary lines, `ERROR collecting`, `errors during collection`, `ImportError while importing test module`), `assertion` (`FAILED … assert` / `AssertionError`), `unknown` (any other red), None (green or timeout). Verified against real `pytest -q` output shapes captured live (module-level missing import → `ERROR tests/test_x.py`; failing assert → `FAILED … - assert 41 == 42`; runtime `TypeError` → unknown). Recorded per provable ref in `L1Report.red_reasons` and serialized in `to_dict()`; the verdict never reads it — the whole F-08 suite passed unchanged.
  - **Prompt sources vendored (AC-8):** `prompts/vendor/mattpocock-skills/` holds `tdd-SKILL.md`/`tdd-mocking.md`/`tdd-tests.md`, `diagnosing-bugs-SKILL.md`, `code-review-SKILL.md` fetched from raw.githubusercontent.com at `49dd158d1076134a641b33efb035946536778336`, plus the upstream MIT `LICENSE` and `SOURCE_SHA` (the full 40-hex commit). The seat prompts adapt them per the WO: implementer = `tdd` (red before green, one test + minimal code per cycle, vertical slices, anti-patterns, mocks at system boundaries only, refactoring belongs to review) + `diagnosing-bugs` Phase 1 for bug tasks (reproduction command before any hypothesis; no correct seam → reported, not papered over); reviewer = `code-review` (two axes + the Fowler ch. 3 smell baseline, issue-tracker lookup dropped — the contract is passed in) + SoloAdventurer's "default FAIL, evidence flips it" + the VALIDATOR-SPEC L2 mandatory checklist and test-quality checks. The "confirm the seams with the user" step became "the contract's `test` refs are the agreed seams; the session is AFK."
  - **Schemas (AC-6):** `project.schema.json` gained optional top-level `standards: [string]` (repo-relative standards docs, reviewer-only per TASK-CONTRACT §5); `tasks.schema.json` gained optional per-task `out_of_scope: [string]` (approval-time scope-creep list for the L2 spec axis; not part of `acceptance_hash`). Both remain `additionalProperties: false` — unknown keys still rejected, proven by the two `pytest.raises(ContractError)` halves of `test_ac_6`.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f09.py::test_ac_1` — prose output → `fail` with `invalid_verdict_json` + `parse_error`; extras cover fenced/brace-span extraction, missing verdict key, uppercase normalisation, and a 7-case malformed matrix (findings not a list, non-object finding, finding without text, unknown verdict value, non-object top level, null axis).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — `PROMPT_PINS` holds the sha256 of each seat prompt file; any edit without copying the new hash into the pin fails the test by construction.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — the assembled reviewer prompt has a `## Mandatory checklist` section containing real-schema-vs-mock, scope, loading/error/empty/offline with offline-unknown, and the invalidation/cancel/logout lifecycle.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a finding without an axis (and one with a non-`spec`/`standards` axis) makes the whole output invalid → FAIL; a `blocking` finding on either axis (parametrised both ways) fails a declared `pass`; non-blocking findings pass.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — the assembled reviewer prompt contains the standards file's text, the Fowler smell baseline (`Duplicated Code`, `Refused Bequest`) and the test-quality checks (`independent source`); the assembled implementer prompt contains none of them. Extra: no-standards assembly states the baseline runs alone; implementer assembly carries the test command and protected paths but never standards.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — via the real loaders (`load_profile`/`load_tasks`): `standards` and `out_of_scope` accepted, still optional, unknown keys still rejected. Red before the schema change recorded above.
- **AC-7** proven: `test_ac_7` — two real fixture repos through `run_l1`: module-level import of a not-yet-existing module → verdict `pass` with `red_reasons == "collection"`; failing assert on base → `pass` with `"assertion"`; `to_dict()` carries `red_reasons`. Extras: the classification matrix on captured real pytest outputs (incl. runtime `TypeError` → `unknown`, green → None, timeout → None), and a proves-nothing fixture → verdict `fail` with `red_reasons` None (verdict independent of the reason).
- **AC-8** proven: `test_ac_8` — every `prompts/vendor/*` source dir carries a non-empty upstream `LICENSE` and a `SOURCE_SHA` matching a 40-hex commit SHA, and vendors ≥1 content file.
- **Wiring (no AC):** `build_options`/`run_session`/`run_session_sandboxed`/spec/`_main` carry `system_prompt` end to end; proven by `test_build_options_carries_an_assembled_system_prompt`, the seat-default-None case, and `test_run_session_forwards_the_system_prompt` (injected query captures the options).
- **Evidence:** 28 F-09 tests / 280 total passing locally (no model spend — F-09 is pure orchestrator-side machinery), ruff clean.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-09 owner review round (PR #19; 8 findings — 7 owner-reproduced locally against `bededb4`, 1 code-reading; all fixed on this PR, red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review8`)

- **(1) Standards files never loaded for real projects** (owner-reproduced; re-verified here): every real `project.yaml` writes `repo.path` as `~/Desktop/...` and assembly never expanded the `~`, so the first real project with `standards:` would fail every L2 review; the tests passed only because they used absolute temp paths. Fix: `_repo_root()` expanduser+resolve's the repo path before joining. Proven by `test_review1_standards_load_when_repo_path_uses_a_tilde` (HOME monkeypatched to the fixture; `~/proj` + `docs/STANDARDS.md` loads into the prompt).
- **(2) A standards entry could read any file into the model prompt** (owner-reproduced; re-verified both ways — a secret file landed in the assembled prompt via an absolute entry AND via `../`): standards are repo-relative by contract (TASK-CONTRACT §5), so `_standards_path` now refuses absolute entries outright and resolves each entry (symlinks included) requiring it to stay inside the resolved repo root; `read_text` is wrapped so an unreadable standards file is a `PromptError`, never a raw OSError. Proven by `test_review2_standards_cannot_read_outside_the_repo` (absolute, `../` and an in-repo symlink pointing outside each refuse; the legit entry still loads).
- **(3) `parse_verdict(None)` crashed** (owner-reproduced): a failed or timed-out reviewer session records `result=None`, and the parser raised AttributeError — taking the orchestrator down instead of stopping the change. Fix: non-string input returns the AC-1 FAIL with `parse_error` naming the session shape. Proven by `test_review3_parse_verdict_none_is_a_fail_not_a_crash`.
- **(4) Unknown severities passed** (owner-reproduced for `blocker`/`critical`/`high`): only the exact word `blocking` blocked, though the seat prompt documents four values. Fix: `severity` is now required and must be exactly one of `blocking`, `major`, `minor`, `note` (case-insensitive; anything else invalidates the verdict per AC-1). The reviewer prompt states this explicitly, and its content pin was updated through the sanctioned pin flow. Proven by `test_review4_unknown_severities_are_invalid_verdicts` (4 values), `test_review4_missing_severity_is_invalid_too`, `test_review4_severity_case_is_normalised` (`BLOCKING` still blocks, validly).
- **(5) The most common red-first shape was labelled `unknown`** (owner-reproduced): a test importing or calling a function that doesn't exist yet fails at RUNTIME (NameError / module-has-no-attribute / ModuleNotFoundError / cannot-import-name), not at collection — yet it is epistemically the same class: red only because a name was missing, the exact case the reviewer is told to scrutinise. Fix: `_RED_NAME_MISSING` joins the collection bucket (assertion checked first, so an assert whose message *quotes* a `NameError:` still classifies as `assertion`; `'NoneType' object has no attribute` stays `unknown` — a genuine runtime bug). Proven by `test_review5_runtime_missing_name_reds_are_collection` (real fixture repo through `run_l1`: import inside the test body → `collection`, verdict untouched) and `test_review5_missing_name_classification_matrix` (4 name-missing shapes → `collection`; genuine TypeError → `unknown`; quoted-NameError assert → `assertion`).
- **(6) An earlier quoted JSON snippet won over the real verdict** (owner-reproduced): candidate order was document-order, so an example verdict quoted in prose parsed before the real one at the end — a false FAIL. Fix: extraction tries the whole text first (a pure-JSON reply), then every fenced block and brace-balanced object **from last to first** (`_balanced_objects` is a string-aware scanner, so braces inside JSON strings don't break spans; nested finding dicts yield one outer span). Proven by `test_review6_the_real_verdict_wins_over_an_earlier_example` (example-fail-then-real-pass → pass, and the symmetric example-pass-then-real-fail → fail, incl. a raw trailing object).
- **(7) Duplicate JSON keys silently flipped the verdict** (owner-reproduced): `json.loads` keeps the last duplicate, so `{"verdict":"fail", …, "verdict":"pass"}` parsed as pass. Fix: `object_pairs_hook` rejects repeated keys at any depth; a dup'd candidate is malformed (skipped like any parse failure — if it's the only object, the verdict is the AC-1 FAIL). Proven by `test_review7_duplicate_keys_are_an_invalid_verdict` (dup verdict key, and a dup inside a finding).
- **(8) The reviewer seat couldn't run Bash headless** (code-reading, confirmed): default permission mode asks someone to approve each call, and an unattended session has nobody to ask — the checks the reviewer spec promises would hang or die. Fix: the reviewer seat now runs `permission_mode="bypassPermissions"` like the implementer — same D3 reasoning (the srt sandbox is the permission system); its read-only-ness comes from the disallowed Write/Edit/NotebookEdit tools, the PreToolUse hooks (protected paths, push/gh ban) and the sandbox's write/network confinement, documented at the seat and in the module docstring. Proven by `test_review8_reviewer_seat_runs_headless` (seat + built options carry the bypass and the disallowed edit tools); the whole F-06 suite stayed green through the change.
- **Test-side note:** two pre-review extra tests (`test_fenced_json_is_extracted`, `test_declared_fail_stays_fail`) omitted `severity`, which finding 4's fix makes a required field — they now carry valid severities (their purpose — fence extraction, declared-fail — is unchanged). The AC tests were untouched.
- **Evidence (after the review round):** 42 F-09 tests / 294 total passing locally, ruff clean (no model spend — parser/assembly/seat changes only).

### F-09 owner review round 2 (PR #19; 1 new finding — fixing round-1 finding 8 let the reviewer WRITE the repo it reviews; fixed on this PR, red-first under `test_review9*`)

- **The hole (owner-reproduced; re-verified here at the sandbox layer against `51b230c`):** finding 8's `bypassPermissions` made the reviewer's Bash headless — but both seats shared the same srt settings, whose `allowWrite: [<run_dir>]` made the whole run dir writable. Under the reviewer's settings, `echo broken > src/app.py` exited 0 (file overwritten), a `python -c` write exited 0, and `git add -A; git commit` exited 0 with HEAD moved — the docstring's "reads, never writes" was false. A reviewer that can write the repo can "fix" the code, watch its own fix pass, and report PASS on a diff that isn't the implementer's; its commit would ride the branch F-10 pushes.
- **Fix layer 1 — read-only reviewer sandbox:** `build_settings(..., read_only=True)` narrows `allowWrite` to exactly the git-excluded `.home`/`.tmp` scratch dirs (created up front so the settings are self-sufficient); the `.git` denyWrite entries stay for both modes; cache writes tools make in the tree (`__pycache__`, `.pytest_cache`) fail closed — Python and pytest degrade to warnings on unwritable caches, so read-only checks still run. `run_session_sandboxed` passes `read_only=(seat == "reviewer")`. Proven by `test_review9_reviewer_sandbox_is_read_only` (real srt: overwrite-source, sed-a-test, python-c-write and git-commit probes all denied, files/HEAD unchanged, `.home`/`.tmp` scratch still writable) and `test_review9_implementer_sandbox_still_writes` (the guard is the reviewer's, not global). `test_review9_session_builds_read_only_settings_for_the_reviewer` pins the wiring (`read_only` True for the reviewer build, False for the implementer's).
- **Fix layer 2 — orchestrator-side integrity backstop (per the owner's two-part direction):** after EVERY reviewer session, hardened-git compares `(rev-parse HEAD, status --porcelain)` against a baseline taken before the session (the scratch dirs are mkdir'd before the baseline so their untracked presence can never masquerade as a mutation). Any difference → the workspace is restored (`git reset --hard <baseline-head>` + `git clean -fd`, no `-x`, so excluded scratch survives) and the record is forced to `failed` with reason `reviewer_mutated_workspace` — the verdict text of a reviewer that changed the code is kept for forensics but never trusted. This is the backstop for any future path that lets a write through (a settings bug, an srt regression). Proven by `test_review9_workspace_integrity_checked_after_review` (a faked session commits + drops an untracked file → failed/`reviewer_mutated_workspace`, HEAD restored, tree clean, smuggled file gone) and `test_review9_clean_review_passes_the_integrity_check` (a quiet review returns untouched; the implementer seat is never subjected to the guard).
- **Docstrings made true:** the reviewer seat's "reads, never writes" is now three enforced layers (disallowed edit tools, read-only sandbox, integrity guard) — module docstring, seat docs and `run_session_sandboxed`'s Returns/Raises all say so, including the `SandboxError`-free git-state failure path (`SessionConfigError` when the baseline can't be read).
- **Evidence (after review round 2):** 47 F-09 tests / 299 total passing locally, ruff clean (real srt sandbox exercised on macOS; CI runs the same tests for real on Linux, no skip path).
- **CI round (Linux-only, found on run 39070829050's re-run; root-caused on a local bwrap 0.8.0 container with SRT_DEBUG + a bwrap argv shim):** the round's first Linux push failed — with the scratch dirs inside the run dir, `test_review9_reviewer_sandbox_is_read_only`'s scratch write got `Read-only file system` while every repo write was denied. Root cause, proven from srt's own emitted mount sequence: our standard `denyRead` includes `runs_root` (sibling-run isolation, an F-05 invariant pinned by `test_sibling_run_reads_are_denied`), so srt mounts a tmpfs over `runs/` and then **restores the run dir read-only (`--ro-bind <run> <run>`) AFTER the writable `.home`/`.tmp` sub-directory binds — burying them**; sub-directory-only `allowWrite` under a read-denied ancestor is read-only on Linux (macOS's backend doesn't stack that way, which is why the test passed locally). Fix: the reviewer's writable scratch now lives OUTSIDE the run dir — `<settings>-scratch/{home,tmp}`, a sibling of the settings file like the staged runner — and `run/.home`/`run/.tmp` are symlinks to it (`_reviewer_scratch`; both spellings ride in `allowWrite` via `build_settings(..., writable_roots=...)`, so writes resolve to the live external mount on Linux and match either spelling on macOS; HOME/TMPDIR paths and the F-05 `/.home/`+`/.tmp/` excludes are unchanged). `cleanup_run` removes the `-scratch` sibling. Verified on bwrap Linux with the exact CI nesting before pushing: scratch writes exit 0; overwrite-source, python-write and `git commit` all denied EROFS (git commit dies at `.git/index.lock`); HEAD unchanged. (The first push of this round also had an infra-flake hang at an F-05 test on one runner — canceled at 48% with orphaned clone processes, clean on re-run — recorded here because the re-run surfaced the real failure above.)
- **Evidence (CI round fixed):** 47 F-09 tests / 299 total passing locally (macOS) AND the 5 review9 tests inside the bwrap Linux container; ruff clean.

### F-09 owner review round 3 (PR #19; 2 findings from the round-2 symlink scratch, both owner-reproduced in the real sandbox; fixed on this PR, red-first under `test_review10*`)

- **(1) The scratch symlinks would get committed** (owner-reproduced; re-verified here): the F-05 exclude patterns are `/.home/`+`/.tmp/`, and a trailing slash matches directories only — a symlink is a file to git, so the reviewer's scratch links showed as `?? .home`/`?? .tmp` and the next `git add -A` would commit two dangling links into the target repo's PR. Fix: `_seed_run_excludes` now seeds `/.home`+`/.tmp` (no trailing slash — matches real dirs AND symlinks; the docstring records why). Proven by `test_review10_excludes_cover_symlinked_scratch` (symlinked scratch → clean porcelain; red on the old patterns) with `test_review10_excludes_still_cover_real_scratch_dirs` guarding the other form. The F-05 behavioral exclude tests (`test_home_and_tmp_scratch_are_git_excluded`, `test_cache_excludes_are_anchored_to_the_run_root`) pass unchanged.
- **(2) A later implementer run in the same run dir lost its home/temp** (owner-reproduced; re-verified by code-reading): the symlinks outlived the review, pointing outside the next sandbox — writes to `$HOME`/`$TMPDIR` were denied while source writes worked, breaking pip/npm/pytest temp dirs and Claude Code's own state for F-10's fix rounds in a reused run dir. Fix: `_teardown_reviewer_scratch` — the inverse of `_reviewer_scratch` — replaces the links with plain `.home`/`.tmp` directories, and runs in a `finally` around the whole sandboxed run so EVERY reviewer exit path (completion, record-parse failure, wall-clock timeout) leaves the run dir in the standard F-05 shape. Proven by `test_review10_review_leaves_real_scratch_dirs` (real dirs, not links, after the review; red on the residue) and `test_review10_implementer_scratch_writable_after_a_review` — the owner's exact repro shape: after a review, a real implementer-settings sandboxed write to `.home` AND `.tmp` in the same run dir exits 0.
- **Evidence (after round 3):** 51 F-09 tests / 303 total passing locally (macOS) and the 9 review9+review10 tests inside the bwrap Linux container; ruff clean; no API spend.

### F-09 owner review round 4 (follow-up PR off main after #19 merged; 3 findings in the round-3 backstop, 2 owner-reproduced, all fixed red-first)

- **(1) The integrity backstop was skipped on the failure paths** (owner-reproduced, and red here): the HEAD/tree comparison and restore ran only after a record parsed, so a reviewer that committed and then timed out, or exited with no record, left its commit in place. The comparison and restore moved into a `finally` around the whole reviewer run; on an exception path the workspace is restored and the original exception still propagates. `test_review11_backstop_restores_when_the_review_times_out`, `test_review11_backstop_restores_when_the_entry_returns_no_record`.
- **(2) Scratch symlinks leaked when setup failed** (owner-reproduced, and red here): `_reviewer_scratch` ran before the `try`, so a `build_settings`/`write_settings`/staging failure left `.home`/`.tmp` as links. Scratch creation and the baseline now sit inside the same `try`; the setup and run were extracted into `_run_prepared`. `test_review11_scratch_torn_down_when_setup_fails`.
- **(3) The check saw only HEAD and porcelain** (owner code-reading; red here): a new branch or tag, a branch switch onto the same commit, a stash and files written into gitignored paths were invisible. `_GitState` now also records the symbolic HEAD, every ref (`for-each-ref`, stash included) and the individual ignored files (`ls-files -o -i --exclude-standard`). `_restore_workspace` deletes created refs, restores moved ones, re-points HEAD, resets, cleans, and removes only ignored files that are new since the baseline, so a warm cache survives. `test_review11_new_branch_counts_as_mutation`, `test_review11_branch_switch_counts_as_mutation`, `test_review11_ignored_artifact_counts_as_mutation`; `test_review11_quiet_review_with_preexisting_ignored_files_passes` guards against false positives. Also checked on a real `create_run` clone with a 2,000-file warm cache: a quiet review passes in 0.27s, cache intact, status clean.
- **Evidence (after round 4):** 58 F-09 tests / 310 total passing locally, ruff clean. No sandbox settings changed (all orchestrator-side git), so the Linux bwrap behaviour from round 2 is unaffected; CI covers Linux.

## F-10 — `factory run` / `factory loop` + PR creation

- **Commits:** `feat/f10-run-loop` → PR (this one). Provenance, recorded honestly: the implementation arrived **uncommitted** in the first F-10 session's worktree (five new modules, five modified, `tests/test_f10.py`); this second session reviewed it module-by-module and verified it (full suite, ruff, AC-by-AC test read) before committing. No intermediate commits exist, so — unlike F-09 — **no red-first run is captured on the branch**; the first recorded execution here was already green, with the 310 pre-F-10 tests unchanged and passing alongside. The suite's per-AC coverage below is what was verified, not what was watched being written. (Superseded at review time: the review round's L1 run — finding 5 below — now pins all 14 refs red on base `e674eef`, `red_reason` `collection`.)
- **Built:** 2026-10-18.
- **Design notes:**
  - **The run (`factory/orchestrator.py`):** reclaim orphans → pick → peak-window check → lease + `in_progress` → clone at the default branch → branch `factory/<slug>-<run_id>` → rounds. A round: implementer session (contract + progress log 1..N in the prompt) → the orchestrator commits the working tree with hardened git (`--no-verify`, factory identity) and **voids the round** (`history_rewritten`) if HEAD no longer descends from the base — the agent owns its clone's history, the orchestrator never pushes a rewrite → fresh clean clone at head → L0 + L1 through the sandboxed runner → L2 reviewer session → FAIL's numbered findings append to the progress log and the next round starts. Escalations per VALIDATOR-SPEC: L0 protected/new-dependency/risk-path hits escalate immediately (`needs_human`, one attempt, most-severe-first); quota/429 **aborts** the attempt (excluded from the budget), re-queues the task and stops the loop; `attempts_exhausted` after `budget.max_attempts` (counted across runs per slice, `aborted` excluded); the wall-clock budget spans a run's implementer+reviewer seconds and escalates `budget_exhausted` rather than starting a session that can't finish. A crashed orchestrator re-queues an `in_progress` task with no recorded outcome (the aborted-run edge), so the queue can't wedge.
  - **Project commands are untrusted (AC-11):** `factory/gates.py` gained the `CommandRunner` seam; `run_l0`/`run_l1` take `runner=`; the orchestrator passes `sandboxed_runner(clean_run_dir, settings)` — every setup/lint/typecheck/test/test_ref command runs through `run_sandboxed` under F-05 settings for the clean clone, and the runner refuses any repo that isn't its own run dir. The gates' default stays the unconfined `run_command` for fixture tests, documented as such at the seam.
  - **The push fence (AC-13, `factory/publish.py`):** `push_branch` pushes exactly one ref by explicit `refs/heads/X:refs/heads/X` to the profile's remote **URL** (never a configured remote name, so no `remote.*.push`/mirror config applies), `--no-follow-tags`, no force. It refuses — before any git process starts — the default branch, anything outside the `factory/` namespace, any branch the run didn't create (a `created` set), and anything that isn't a plain branch name (refspec syntax, `+`, `HEAD`, `..`, `.lock`). `gh_pr_create` opens the PR (body via a temp file outside every clone — argv limits and `ps`); the factory never merges (AC-5's grep test pins it). `gates.git` and L1's `cat-file` are hardened too: after F-10, **every** orchestrator-side git call carries `GIT_HARDENING`.
  - **The PR body (AC-7/8/9/14, `factory/prbody.py`):** six sections — Summary, Evidence, Merge Danger, Contract, Validator report, How to revert — and `check_pr_body` refuses to open a PR missing or duplicating any. Evidence/Door/Blast Radius/How-to-revert are built from validator facts only (L1's per-ref red_reasons on base; risk flags + matched `paths.risk` globs or `contained`; Door `one-way` when the task carries `migration`/`deploy`/`external_service`/`payments`/`privacy`, else `two-way`; per-Door revert text naming each flag a revert can't undo). The implementer's account rides below as inert fenced text; command tails shrink in three passes to fit GitHub's 65,536-char body limit, then the gate still applies.
  - **The progress log (AC-10, `factory/progress.py`):** one append-only jsonl per task under `<factory_root>/progress/<project>/<task>.jsonl` — outside every clone, fcntl-locked, hash-chained (each entry carries its number and the sha256 of the previous line; there is no edit API). Every new session — re-queued run or future fix round — receives entries 1..N rendered read-only in its prompt; rewriting, dropping or reordering an earlier entry raises on read and on the next append.
  - **Cleanup (AC-12):** `cleanup_run` runs in `finally` for both run dirs (work clone + each gate clone) on every outcome — PR opened, `needs_human`, quota stop, exception. `reclaim_runs` removes every run dir of the project **under the new `idle_lease`** (no live run can race it; a killed run's dirs are orphans the moment the lease is free) plus stray `-runner`/`-scratch` siblings under `<root>/sandbox/`, at the start of every `run_once` — so `loop` reclaims what a killed run left.
  - **The peak window (AC-4, `factory/config.py`):** `<factory_root>/config.yaml`, default `Europe/Stockholm` Mon–Fri 08:00–12:00 (end exclusive) — the owner's working hours, from config not code. Checked before the implementer session *and* before the reviewer session (a window reached mid-run stops before the next session and re-queues). Malformed config is a `ConfigError`, not a default: a silently mis-parsed window would spend quota at exactly the wrong hours. DST is real (ZoneInfo; the boundary test crosses Stockholm's fall-back).
  - **CLI:** `factory run` / `factory loop` are real subcommands. Exit codes: 0 = PR opened / idle / peak window, 1 = `needs_human`, 3 = quota stop, 2 = the orchestrator itself failed (task re-queued, run dirs removed first) — known failures print a message, not a traceback.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f10.py::test_ac_1` — full `run` on a fixture repo with a stub seat that makes a correct change: branch pushed, PR opened, task `in_review`, one attempt logged.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — a stub failing three times → `needs_human` + `attempts_exhausted`, no PR. Extras: a *session* failure counts as a failed attempt (`test_session_failure_counts_as_a_failed_attempt`); attempts already spent in earlier runs count toward the budget (`test_budget_counts_earlier_attempts`); a failing reviewer is retried then exhausts (`test_reviewer_fail_is_retried_then_exhausted`); an L0 escalation stops immediately without burning retries (`test_l0_escalation_stops_immediately`).
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — a 429-named implementer failure stops `loop` (`quota_exhausted`), attempt recorded `aborted`, task re-queued `ready`, no retry; `test_reviewer_quota_also_stops` — the same for a reviewer 429 mid-run.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — `loop` starts no session inside the window (config-supplied `Europe/Stockholm` hours); `test_peak_window_boundaries_and_dst` — inclusive start, exclusive end, DST-correct across the Stockholm fall-back; `test_bad_config_is_refused` — malformed window → `ConfigError`; `test_peak_window_reached_mid_run_stops_before_next_session` — a window reached between implementer and reviewer stops before the reviewer session.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — four merge-call patterns (`gh pr merge`, `pr, merge` argv, the REST merge endpoint, `merge --`) find no match across `factory/**/*.py` **and** `prompts/**/*.md`.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — every attempt carries `prompt_versions` (F-09 seat → assembled-prompt hashes) and `skills`; an attempt without them fails the tasks schema.
- **AC-7** proven: `test_ac_7` — the opened PR's body has all five AC-7 sections, the real body passes `check_pr_body`, and renaming/removing any section, or appending a forged duplicate, raises before a PR opens.
- **AC-8** proven: `test_ac_8` — the Evidence section is built from the L1 report: each contract ref shows red on base (with its `red_reason`) and green on head; `test_ac_8_collection_red_reason` — a collection-error red carries its reason through.
- **AC-9** proven: `test_ac_9` — Door is `one-way` exactly for the five risk classes (and risk flags L0 added at runtime), `two-way` otherwise; Blast Radius lists flags + matched `paths.risk` globs or `contained`; implementer prose lands below, never alters either value.
- **AC-10** proven: `test_ac_10` — a fresh session's prompt carries entries 1..N in order, the log lives outside every clone, and rewriting an earlier entry raises on read and next append; `test_progress_log_reaches_every_round` — each new round of the same run receives the log too.
- **AC-11** proven: `test_ac_11` — L0 and L1 execute every profile command and each `test_ref` through `run_sandboxed` on the clean clone: a fixture `conftest.py` that writes outside the run dir and reads `~/factory/holdouts` is denied both at the L0 gate and in L1's red/green runs.
- **AC-12** proven: `test_ac_12` — no run dir, settings, staged runner or scratch survives any outcome (PR, `needs_human`, quota, exception); `test_ac_12_killed_run_is_cleaned_by_next_loop` — a killed run's dirs are reclaimed by the next run's start.
- **AC-13** proven: `test_ac_13` — pushes to the default branch, refs the run didn't create, and non-plain names all raise **before any git process starts** (every `Popen` is recorded; none may carry the bad ref), and every orchestrator git argv carries `GIT_HARDENING`; `test_agent_moving_the_default_branch_in_its_clone_is_never_pushed` — an agent that moves `main` inside its clone changes nothing the orchestrator pushes.
- **AC-14** proven: `test_ac_14` — two-way → "revert the squash-merge commit"; one-way → a statement that reverting doesn't undo the named risk flags (task risks and L0-added flags both); an implementer's own revert section is replaced, not trusted; no PR without the section.
- **Wiring/extras (no AC):** `test_gh_pr_create_wrapper` (real wrapper contract: refusals, temp-file body, URL extraction), `test_default_seat_runner_signature_matches` (the default seat is the real `run_session_sandboxed`, signature-pinned), `test_huge_output_still_fits_a_pr_body`, `test_cli_run_and_loop_are_wired`, `test_nothing_to_run_is_idle`.
- **Evidence:** 32 F-10 tests / 342 total passing locally (macOS; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean. CI runs the same suite for real on 3.11 + 3.13.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-10 owner review round (PR #22; 5 findings — 2 owner-reproduced with the PR's own `Env` harness, 2 correctness, 1 conventions; all fixed in `bedf7ea`, red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review4`)

- **(1) The push published the branch ref, not the validated commit** (owner-reproduced): `push_branch` pushed `refs/heads/X:refs/heads/X` from the agent-owned work clone, so whatever the branch pointed at *at push time* went public — a surviving implementer process (F-06 documents setsid escapees that outlive the killpg teardown) could commit after L0/L1/L2 validated `head_sha`, and the remote would hold the rewrite while the PR body's Evidence described the validated commit. Fix: the refspec is now `<sha>:refs/heads/<branch>` — the source is the object name, so publishing is immune to branch movement — and after the push, `ls-remote` must read the ref back as exactly that sha or the publish fails before `gh pr create` (an unconfirmed publish never becomes a PR). `sha` is a required argument validated as a full object name before any git process starts. Proven by `test_review1_push_publishes_the_validated_sha_not_the_branch` (the reviewer's exact repro: a commit lands on the work branch between validation and push; old code published `UNVALIDATED`, new code publishes the validated change) and `test_review1_push_confirms_the_remote_ref_or_refuses` (a lying ls-remote → `PublishError`). AC-13's two refspec assertions updated to the stronger contract: source is the 40-hex object and the remote rev-parses to exactly it.
- **(2) Orchestrator errors recorded no attempt, so a persistent failure looped forever at full cost** (owner-reproduced): every exception out of `_run_task` re-queued the task with zero attempts recorded — a `gh pr create` 422 after a successful push, a gate crash, a reviewer timeout — so the attempt budget never decreased, both seats re-ran per `loop` invocation, and pushed branches piled up unnamed on the remote. Fix: the round body is wrapped; an ordinary `Exception` now records an escalated attempt from whatever state exists (sessions, reports, tokens) and either (a) after the push — the branch is already public — goes `needs_human` with escalation `pr_create_failed` and the branch kept in `links`, or (b) before the push, re-queues `ready` with the attempt spent, so a persistent failure is bounded by `attempts_exhausted` instead of looping forever. `BaseException` (a hard kill) still escapes un-recorded — that is the killed-run path `reclaim_runs` covers; `KeyboardInterrupt` proves the exception-path cleanup half of AC-12 now. Schema: `pr_create_failed` and `orchestrator_error` joined the escalation enum, red-first per the F-09 pattern (`test_review2_new_escalations_are_schema_valid` fails against the pre-round schema). Proven by `test_review2_pr_failure_after_push_escalates_instead_of_requeueing` (the reviewer's repro shape: open_pr 422 → `needs_human`, attempt `escalated`/`pr_create_failed` with the note, branch kept and named) and `test_review2_orchestrator_error_records_attempts_and_bounds_the_loop` (persistent L1 crash → error attempts → `attempts_exhausted`, loop stops, nothing ever pushed). AC-7's orchestrator-path half updated to the new semantics: a body missing a section is an `error` outcome with the attempt recorded — the invariant (no PR, nothing pushed) is unchanged.
- **(3) A peak-window stop before the L2 review silently discarded a validated round** (correctness): the implementer's session, tokens and wall clock vanished — no attempt, no log entry — and the next run redid the work from zero, under-counting cost (AC-6, F-19 credit assignment). Fix: the stop now records an `aborted` attempt (the window is nobody's fault — it doesn't burn the budget) carrying the implementer's tokens and a note naming the validated head, and the progress log tells the next session the change existed, passed L0 and L1, and was discarded with the run. Deferred, deliberately: true commit resumption (keeping the work clone alive across runs) conflicts with AC-12's cleanup-every-outcome and the unique-branch-per-run design; what the redo needs survives in the attempt note and the on-disk L0/L1 reports. Proven by `test_review3_peak_window_before_review_records_the_aborted_round` (clock crosses into the window between implementer and reviewer: `aborted` attempt with `input_tokens` 120, note and rendered log both name the validated head and its L0/L1 pass).
- **(4) The reviewer could start on a spent wall clock** (correctness): `timeout_min=max(remaining, 1.0)` always granted the reviewer at least a minute past the budget the implementer had already consumed, and `session_seconds` was never re-checked. Fix: the remaining budget is computed and checked before the reviewer session starts — ≤ 0 escalates `budget_exhausted` (attempt recorded, `needs_human`), exactly as the implementer path does; otherwise the reviewer gets precisely the remainder (no floor). Proven by `test_review4_reviewer_not_started_when_the_budget_is_spent` (a faked monotonic clock spends the budget in the implementer; the reviewer is never invoked and the run escalates `budget_exhausted`).
- **(5) No red-first evidence for AC-1..AC-14** (conventions; the main entry's Commits line records why). Fixed by dogfooding F-08: `run_l1` over F-10's contract refs with base `e674eef` (main, pre-F-10) and head `bedf7ea` — run on a scratch clone whose head is padded with the pinned toolchain (`node_modules` force-added as one commit; `run_l1`'s inter-phase `git clean -fdx` removes an ignored install, which is the same reason CI runs `npm ci`; verified the padded head `a14414e` differs from `bedf7ea` by `node_modules/` paths only, so the red phase — which applies only test-file changes — is unaffected). **L1 verdict `pass`: all 14 refs red on base (exit 4, `red_reason` `collection` — the test module imports `factory.orchestrator`/`publish`/`prbody`/`progress`/`config`, absent at base) and green on head (exit 0).** The full per-ref table: every `tests/test_f10.py::test_ac_N`, N=1..14, red_reason `collection`. This pins, at review time, that each AC test genuinely fails without the F-10 code — late, but produced by the machinery the factory itself will use.
- **Evidence (after the review round):** 7 new review tests; 39 F-10 tests / 349 total passing locally (macOS; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean.

### F-10 owner review round 2 (PR #22; 3 findings in the round-1 fixes, 2 owner-reproduced with the `Env` harness, 1 conventions; all fixed red-first under `test_review5`–`test_review7`)

- **(1) A bookkeeping failure after the PR opened led to a second PR** (owner-reproduced on `fc7f3fc`): the pass attempt and PR URL were saved, then the `in_review` transition failed, `run_once`'s outer handler re-queued the task to `ready`, and the next run ran both seats again and opened PR #2. New `_finish_unrecorded_publish` runs first in every run: when the slice's latest attempt is a `pass` with a `pr` that `links.pr` doesn't match, it writes `links.pr`, moves the task to `in_review` and returns `pr_opened` without starting a session. A fix round after a rejected PR has matching `links.pr`, so it isn't caught. `test_review5_bookkeeping_failure_after_pr_never_opens_a_second_pr`: one failed `in_review` transition, then a second run; 1 PR total, 2 seat calls total, status `in_review`.
- **(2) Push-fence and PR-body refusals were retried until the budget ran out** (owner-reproduced: 6 seat sessions over 3 runs): `PushRefused` and `PRBodyError` before the push now escalate at once with the new `publish_refused` reason (`needs_human`), and `loop` now stops on an `error` outcome. A persistent orchestrator error spends one attempt per `loop` invocation and still ends in `attempts_exhausted`. Tests: `test_review6_push_fence_refusal_escalates_immediately`, `test_review6_pr_body_refusal_escalates_immediately`, `test_review6_loop_stops_on_an_orchestrator_error`. Two earlier tests changed to the new semantics: AC-7's orchestrator half (a missing section is now `publish_refused` → `needs_human`; still no PR, nothing pushed), and the round-1 bounded-loop test (four `loop` calls stop `error`, `error`, `error`, `escalation`).
- **(3) The new escalation reasons weren't swept into TASK-CONTRACT.md** (conventions, owner's global CLAUDE.md "sweep every surface"): line 79 now lists `pr_create_failed`, `orchestrator_error` and `publish_refused`, each with the owner action. The rule is a test, `test_review7_task_contract_lists_every_escalation_reason`, so the schema and the doc can't drift again.
- **Evidence (after review round 2):** 5 new review tests, all red against `fc7f3fc` for the reported reasons; 44 F-10 tests / 354 total passing locally, ruff clean.

### Rehearsal fixes (JobFinderOS readiness run, 2026-10-18; F-05, F-08, F-10)

A readiness run of the factory's own gates on a clean clone of JobFinderOS (`origin/main` `9daedd1`, every command inside srt) found three factory bugs that the fixture repos couldn't show. Each is fixed with a test that was red first.

- **(1) L1 deleted what setup installed (F-08):** the between-phase `git clean -fdx` removed `backend/.venv` and `frontend/node_modules`, so every contract ref failed with exit 127 in both phases and a healthy test read as `test_not_green`. A new optional profile key `paths.setup_outputs` names those directories, and `_clean` keeps them (each excluded as an anchored path) while still wiping everything else, `__pycache__` included (the F-08 bytecode test still passes). `tests/test_f08.py::test_rehearsal_clean_keeps_declared_setup_outputs`. On JobFinderOS, with `setup_outputs: [backend/.venv, frontend/node_modules]`, the L1 demo ref ran in both phases ("1 passed" each) instead of exit 127.
- **(2) Sandboxed commands inherited home dirs on PATH (F-05):** the ambient PATH starts with `~/.local/bin`, `~/.pyenv/shims` and so on, which the sandbox denies reading. npm's lifecycle spawn hit a denied dir first and died with `spawn EPERM`. `sandbox_env` now drops inherited PATH entries under the home dir (either spelling) and literal `~` entries, keeping order; an explicit caller PATH is left as given. `tests/test_f05.py::test_rehearsal_ambient_path_drops_home_entries` and `::test_rehearsal_sandboxed_child_sees_no_home_path_entries`. On JobFinderOS, `setup` (pip + `npm install`) passed inside the sandbox with no workaround.
- **(3) The run built on the local checkout's stale `main` (F-10):** the work clone came from the local repo at `default_branch`, which was a week behind GitHub (`b0e2417` against `9daedd1`). `_build_on_remote_default` now fetches the remote's default branch with hardened git and detaches there before branching; if the fetch fails the run refuses with `RunError` before any session. `tests/test_f10.py::test_rehearsal_run_builds_on_the_remote_default_branch` and `::test_rehearsal_run_refuses_when_the_remote_is_unreachable`.
- **Evidence:** 5 new tests, all red against `75e8b6d` for the reported reasons; 359 total passing locally, ruff clean. The JobFinderOS readiness run against the fixed code: L0 `pass` in 139s (setup, lint, typecheck, 738 backend tests, dev_lock, flow, frontend_unit, migration_single_head), no escalations, no simulated workarounds.

## F-29 — Owner-step tick (`factory tick`)

- **Commits:** `feat/f29-owner-step-tick`, stacked on `chore/reconcile-bootstrap` (PR #27). Red-first: `tests/test_f29.py` failed at collection on the missing `factory.tick` module, and `test_ac_5` failed on the F-04 tests still pinning F-00 to `draft`.
- **Built:** 2026-10-19. Interactive session, owner-requested after the reconcile left F-06 at `awaiting_owner` and F-09/F-10 refused behind it.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/tick.py` (`tick_steps`, `TickError`) and `factory tick <id> <step...> --evidence <text> [--done-by]`. All refusals run through one shared `state.check_tick` before any confirmation, so a bad step number or blank evidence is caught before you are asked anything. Each step is confirmed on its own (no `--yes`); then `TaskQueue.tick_owner_steps` applies the ticks and any closing transition to a copy of the task and writes once. Two new table edges, `draft -> not_buildable` and `not_buildable -> done`, are guarded by `_check_kind_rules` in `transition`, `transition_path` and the tick path: without the guard a build task could skip validation via `draft -> not_buildable -> done`. Schema: owner steps gain `done_at`, `done_by`, `evidence`, required together with `done: true`; an `owner_only` task that is `done` is exempt from the frozen-contract rule but must have at least one step, every one ticked (schema and `contract.py` agree). Decision and research kinds keep the old rule.
- **Changed existing tests (behaviour changed on purpose):** `tests/test_f03.py` — the illegal `exit_from_not_buildable` class swaps `not_buildable -> done` (now legal for owner_only) for `not_buildable -> in_progress`; the two hand-ticked fixtures carry the new audit fields. `tests/test_f04.py` — the real-files reconcile tests assert F-00 is unchanged by reconcile instead of `draft` (AC-5).
- **Rehearsal on a copy of the real queue:** ticking F-00 steps 1–6 → `done`; F-06 step 1 → `done`; reconcile then took F-09 and F-10 to `done`.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f29.py::test_ac_1` — one confirmation per step naming the step text; the ticked step carries `done_by`, `evidence`, a `Z` timestamp; untouched steps unchanged; `done: true` without audit fields refused at load; CLI ticks two steps with `--done-by`, refuses a missing `--evidence` and any `--yes`.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` (unknown task, step 0 and 3 out of range, blank evidence, declined, one bad step among good ones) plus `test_ac_2_already_ticked` and `test_ac_2_cli_refusal_exits_nonzero` — the file is byte-identical after every refusal; the CLI exits 1 with the reason on stderr.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — `awaiting_owner` stays until its last step, then `done` in the same save; `ready`, `in_progress`, `in_review`, `deploying` record the ticks and keep their status.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` (owner_only from `draft` and from `not_buildable` closes to `done`), `test_ac_4_executable_kind_cannot_enter_not_buildable[build|docs]`, `test_ac_4_executable_kind_cannot_leave_not_buildable_to_done[build|docs]`, `test_ac_4_owner_only_needs_every_step_ticked`, `test_ac_4_owner_only_done_on_disk_needs_ticked_steps`.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — reconcile over copies of the real files leaves every owner_only task identical, and the F-04 real-files tests no longer pin F-00 to `draft`.
- **Owner follow-up after merge:** `factory tick F-00 1 2 3 4 5 6 --evidence "..."`, `factory tick F-06 1 --evidence "..."`, then `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` for F-09, F-10 and this entry.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

## F-24 — Realistic rehearsal fixture in CI

- **Commits:** `feat/f24-rehearsal-fixture` — `feat: realistic rehearsal fixture in CI (F-24)` + `fix: ac_4 asserts the rehearsal lives in the tree CI runs (F-24)` (the dogfood proof caught AC-4 proving nothing on base; see Evidence).
- **Built:** 2026-10-19. Interactive session, per BUILD-ORDER Phase 1b ("hardening WOs are built interactively, because they touch what judges agents").
- **Design notes:** new `factory/rehearsal.py` — the fixture builder + `run_rehearsal`. The fixture is realistic on the three incident axes: `commands.setup` creates a real venv and installs a vendored npm package (lifecycle script included) into `node_modules` — offline, `--no-audit --no-fund`, no registry — with both declared as `paths.setup_outputs`; `test`/`test_ref` run through `.venv/bin/python`, `lint` through `node_modules/.bin`; the bare remote's `main` is one commit ahead of the local clone; `ambient_path_with_home_entries()` recreates the incident's interactive PATH (real home dirs first plus a literal `~` entry). The rehearsal itself is one `run_once` — real clone manager, remote-default fetch, srt-sandboxed L0 and L1, push, PR — with only the seats stubbed (`_RehearsalSeats`): exactly the readiness-run mechanics F-28 step 2 needs. The task budget is `max_attempts: 2` so a reverted fix burns to `needs_human` in one rehearsal, fast.
- **The PATH sensitivity, honestly derived:** the original npm `spawn EPERM` was not deterministically reproducible offline (an empirical spike with home-first PATH — pyenv shims present, node lifecycle spawning — passed both with and without the fix: shell exec search skips denied candidates on macOS). The fix itself also drops literal `~` entries — a real leak class, and one no legitimate resolved PATH ever contains. The fixture's tracked `tools/env-guard.sh` (a plain toolchain precondition) refuses an unexpanded `~` entry: the sanitised child PATH never carries one, a leaked one always does — deterministic on macOS and Linux, inside the real sandbox, with no dependence on execvp error classes. Recorded here so nobody thinks the guard is decorative. **Correction (owner re-check, 2026-10-19):** the spike's conclusion does not hold on the owner's Mac. Run through the full rehearsal path with absolute home entries leaked (filter reduced to `startswith('~')`, old `~`-only guard), setup fails with the real npm EPERM ("operation was rejected by your operating system", exit 255). So the absolute-home leak does reproduce here. The guard stays as the canary that fires on every host, including ones where EPERM doesn't (not verified on the Linux runner).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f24.py::test_ac_1` — remote ahead of local (ancestor-checked in the remote, which holds both objects), `setup_outputs == [.venv, node_modules]`, setup offline by construction (no URL, `--no-audit --no-fund`), ambient PATH carries home entries and a literal `~`; setup then runs end to end through the real sandbox under that PATH and both trees exist afterwards.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — one `run_once` over the one-criterion contract through `run_rehearsal`: `pr_opened`, one PR, task `in_review`, attempt `pass`, L0/L1 `pass` with the red exit ≠ 0 and green exit = 0. The remote-only-commit property is no longer asserted by the test directly — `run_rehearsal` returns only when the outcome is `pr_opened` AND the PR head contains the remote's advanced main (it raises otherwise), so returning at all is the proof (review round 2, finding 1).
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — three monkeypatched reverts, each on a fresh fixture, each expecting a `RehearsalError` carrying the run result (review round 2, finding 1: any non-`pr_opened` outcome raises too): `_clean` without keeps → `needs_human` with L1 `test_not_green` (exit 127 both phases); `_path_outside_home` identity → `needs_human` with L0 `setup_failed`, the guard's own `env-guard:` message asserted in the setup output (review round 2, finding 3: attribution, not just failure); `_build_on_remote_default` no-op → `run_rehearsal` raises on the stale-base PR.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — asserts the rehearsal harness lives in the tree CI clones (`factory/rehearsal.py`, checked as a file so an editable install can't mask its absence), ci.yml triggers on `pull_request`, the pytest step is exactly `pytest -q` with no marker/path filters and no `if`/`continue-on-error`, and `test_ac_1..3` carry no pytest markers or skip attributes.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `docs/incidents.md` (new, with `docs/`) holds one section per incident, each with **What broke** / **Which layer should have caught it** / test refs; every `tests/…::name` ref in the whole file is resolved by `ast.parse` (parsed, never executed — review round 1 finding 7) and verified to exist, so the register can't drift from the suite. The three PR #24 incidents are entries INC-001..003, each citing its original PR #24 test plus `test_ac_3`.
- **Evidence (red-first):** the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.rehearsal` (all five refs). Dogfood `run_l1` over the five contract refs, scratch clone, base `106f051` (main), head padded with `node_modules` as one commit (854 paths, verified to differ by `node_modules/` only): **first run verdict FAIL — `test_ac_4` was `test_proves_nothing` on base** (its assertions were retrospective over files already on main); fixed by asserting the harness file exists, re-run **verdict `pass`**: red `106f051`/green `88bee8a` (padded), exits 1/0 per ref — ac_1 exit 1 unknown, ac_2/ac_3/ac_4 exit 1 assertion, ac_5 exit 1 unknown (the reds ran under the orchestrator interpreter, whose editable install of the branch lets the file collect; on a clean base checkout the file is red at collection on the missing `factory.rehearsal`).
- **Suite:** 5 F-24 tests / 382 total passing locally (macOS, real srt; ~21s for the F-24 file — three full rehearsals in AC-3 included), ruff clean. CI runs the same suite unchanged: the rehearsal is a plain test, no markers, no new CI step.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-24 owner review round (PR #33; 9 findings — all fixed red-first where behaviour changed)

- **(1) PATH revert proof only covered the literal-~ branch** (test coverage): the guard now bakes the host's home paths (both spellings — the same set `_path_outside_home` drops) into `tools/env-guard.sh` and refuses entries under them, so a filter simplified to `startswith('~')` still fails the rehearsal. `test_review1_path_leak_covers_absolute_home_entries` monkeypatches exactly that simplified filter and passes now. **Correction:** it was *not* red-first on the owner's Mac. Against the old `~`-only guard it passes too, because the leaked home entries trigger the real npm EPERM and setup fails anyway (see the F-24 PATH-sensitivity correction above). The test pins the behaviour on every host but does not prove the baked-home guard is needed on macOS.
- **(2) `run_rehearsal` couldn't detect stale-base itself** (altitude): `run_rehearsal` now raises `RehearsalError` when a `pr_opened` PR head lacks the remote's default-branch commit (rc≠0 from merge-base, stderr surfaced in the message) or when no PR was recorded. `test_ac_3`'s third revert expects the `RehearsalError` raise (red before the fix: DID NOT RAISE); `test_ac_2` returns without raising, which is the positive proof; F-28's readiness flow treats a raise as not-ready. A separate `test_review2_run_rehearsal_detects_stale_base` was dropped in a follow-up: it was the same scenario as `test_ac_3`'s third revert and cost one extra full rehearsal.
- **(3) Fixture .gitignore omitted .venv/node_modules** (correctness): a real project ignores what setup installs, and `_commit_work`'s `git add -A` would put both trees into any session's PR. `/.venv/` and `/node_modules/` added; `test_review3_fixture_ignores_install_trees` proves both via `git check-ignore` — red before, green after.
- **(4) Empty ambient PATH yielded a trailing empty entry (= cwd)** (correctness): `ambient_path_with_home_entries` filters empty entries everywhere, including the inherited PATH's own interior empties. `test_review4_ambient_path_has_no_empty_entries` (PATH unset) was red before.
- **(5) Local-clone ancestry assertion was vacuously true** (test correctness): the local-repo merge-base exited 128 (missing object) for any error, which read as "not an ancestor". AC-1 now judges aheadness in the remote under the new strict `_is_ancestor` (rc must be 0 or 1; anything else fails the test with stderr), and checks the local clone really lacks the commit via `cat-file -e` (rc≠0). Not red-first: the fixture behaviour was already correct; the assertion was the bug.
- **(6) AC-4 missed module/conftest/trigger filters** (test coverage): `test_ac_4` additionally asserts no module-level `pytestmark`, no `pytest_collection_modifyitems` in a root/tests conftest (AST scan), no `addopts` under `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`, and no `paths`/`paths-ignore` on the PR trigger. Verified red by temporarily adding `pytestmark = [pytest.mark.slow]` (new assertion failed), then removing it.
- **(7) AC-5 re-executed cited test modules** (correctness): ref resolution is now `_test_names_in` — an `ast.parse` scan for top-level `def`s, zero execution. `test_review7_incident_refs_resolve_without_executing` resolves a poisoned module (module-level raise + a def) that the old exec-based helper crashed on.
- **(8) Every rehearsal rebuilt the fixture and reinstalled** (efficiency): the git fixture (repo + bare remote + advance push) is now built once per session (`fixture_template`) and copied per scenario with the profile's repo paths rewritten; per-scenario state (queue mutations, pushes) stays isolated. Fixture construction is now paid once. The saving is only the git work (init, bare clone, advance push); a per-rehearsal figure from 26s vs 21s wall-clock runs is within noise and is not claimed. The five sandboxed setup installs themselves remain by design: they are the realism F-24 exists to exercise, and test_ac_1's standalone setup is that AC's own direct proof.
- **(9) Four divergent copies of the git helpers** (reuse): `rehearsal.py`'s private `_git` is gone (uses `factory.gates.git`, hardened, like the orchestrator); the test file has one `_git_out`-free shape — `hardened_git(...).strip()` where output is needed — and a single strict `_is_ancestor` (finding 5's semantics); `run_rehearsal`'s internal check is the harness-side strict form (raises with stderr). The vacuous-assertion bug of finding 5 came from exactly the old divergence; both remaining copies now fail loudly on git errors.
- **Evidence (review round):** reds against the pre-fix head `e94faa7` (owner re-check: new tests run against the `e94faa7` `factory/rehearsal.py`): `test_ac_3` (DID NOT RAISE), `test_review3`, `test_review4`, and the since-dropped `test_review2` fail for the reported reasons. `test_review1` **passes** against the old module on macOS (real npm EPERM, see above), so it is not red-first evidence; findings 5–7 and 9 are test/structure fixes proven as described above. After the fixes: 10 F-24 tests / **387 total** passing locally (9 / 386 after `test_review2` was dropped), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` re-run over the five contract refs (base `106f051`, node_modules-padded head): verdict `pass`, red exits 1 / green exits 0 per ref, unchanged.

### F-24 owner review round 2 (PR #33, head `e2357a9`; 9 findings — all fixed)

Now every non-`pr_opened` outcome raises `RehearsalError` (message carries the outcome, escalation and L0/L1/L2 finding ids), and the exception carries the run's `result` and `prs` for inspection. `test_ac_3` cases (1)/(2) and `test_review1` switched to `pytest.raises` — red before the fix (DID NOT RAISE).
- **(2) The PR reply comment was out of date** (conventions): a correction comment is posted on PR #33 retracting the three withdrawn claims (review1 red-first, review2 red, ~25% speedup) and pointing at `e2357a9`'s ledger corrections and this round.

On macOS the leaked PATH also triggers the real npm EPERM (the `e2357a9` correction), so without the message check a broken guard could pass unnoticed; with it the guard itself is the proven canary on every host.

- **(5) AC-4 missed pytest.ini/setup.cfg/tox.ini and ignore hooks** (test coverage): `test_ac_4` now asserts none of `pytest.ini`/`tox.ini`/`setup.cfg` exists (the repo's pytest config lives in pyproject.toml only), and the conftest AST scan also rejects `pytest_ignore_collect` defs and `collect_ignore`/`collect_ignore_glob` assignments. Verified red by planting `pytest.ini` with `addopts = -m 'not slow'` and a `tests/conftest.py` with `collect_ignore = ['tests/test_f24.py']`: the new assertions failed; removed, green.
- **(6) run_ref's missing-name exit contradicted its docstring** (correctness): a typo'd test name raised AttributeError outside the try and exited 1 (reads as `test_not_green`). Now `getattr(module, name, None)` → stderr "no test named …" and exit 2, matching the documented contract. `test_review8_run_ref_missing_name_exits_two` — red before (exit 1), green after; honest exits 0/1 re-verified.
- **(7) Raw git subprocess calls bypassed the hardening** (reuse): both fixture clones and `run_rehearsal`'s merge-base now carry `GIT_HARDENING` (the merge-base keeps raw-subprocess rc semantics — rc 1 IS the answer there — with a comment saying why it isn't `gates.git`).
- **(8) Home paths were interpolated into the guard unescaped** (correctness): `_env_guard_source` bakes the home paths one per line inside a quoted heredoc — literal to the shell whatever the characters — and rejects a home containing a newline at build time. `test_review9_env_guard_survives_odd_home_paths` (HOME set to `/tmp/fa'ke`): `sh -n` clean, the odd-home PATH leaks are refused with the guard message, clean PATHs pass — red against the old interpolation.

After the fixes: 11 F-24 tests / **388 total** passing locally, ruff clean. One intermediate round bug caught by the suite itself: the heredoc rework initially kept `.format`-style doubled braces in the shell template (`${{rest%%:*}}` → bad substitution), failing every setup-dependent test — fixed to verbatim braces with a comment warning `str.format` off.
