- **(2) AC-3's "reporting it ready" was invisible** (correctness / F-28 blocker): F-28's owner step says merge factory PRs "only after `factory run` has reported them ready against the current `main`", but a pass only reached the progress log and an up-to-date PR only stamped the cache. `RunResult.ready_prs` now lists every open PR the sweep proved current — up to date, re-validated now, or cached as either for exactly the current remote head — as `<url> against <default-branch>@<sha12>`, attached to every outcome reached after the sweep; `factory run` prints one `  ready: …` line each, `factory loop` prints them under each run. An unknown-state or failing PR is never reported. `test_review43` (up to date, re-validated after main moves, cached hit, unknown state, stale failure) and `test_review44` (CLI lines for run and loop).
- **Also changed:** `test_review39` asserts `links.merged == links.pr` instead of `is True` (the stamp's new shape); TASK-CONTRACT (`needs_human → in_review` row, `stale_base` paragraph incl. the ready line) and VALIDATOR-SPEC's merge-order paragraph updated to the URL stamp and the ready report.
- **Evidence (round 7):** 3 tests red against `c298ae0` (`test_review42` also confirmed red on the behavior itself — `pr_opened` where `concurrency_wait` was due — not only on the stamp's shape); after the fixes 50 F-26 tests / **492 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` was **not** re-run this round.

## F-27 — Vent tool

- **Commits:** `feat/f27-vent-tool` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.vent` module (`ModuleNotFoundError`, the F-26 pattern), and the dogfood `run_l1` below proves each contract ref red on base with `red_reason: collection` (test file applied to base imports the not-yet-existing module).
- **Built:** 2026-10-22. Interactive session, after a full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER F-27 + the queue's build order, TASK-CONTRACT, VALIDATOR-SPEC, the F-26 ledger incl. all seven review rounds, factory/session.py, progress.py, runs.py, orchestrator.py `_run_task`, sentinels.redact_credentials, both schemas' attempt shape, the f06/f10 test harnesses, the pinned claude-agent-sdk 0.2.168's in-process tool API — `tool` decorator, `create_sdk_mcp_server`, `allowed_tools` as a grant list, `isError` wrapping of handler raises) per the owner's understand-first directive; branched from `origin/main` (`c3fbb54`, F-26 merged; the F-26 reconcile PR #41 was still open at branch time — this PR doesn't touch `tasks.json`, so the two can land in either order).
- **Design notes:** new `factory/vent.py`. The one structural fact the WO forces: the vent handler runs **inside the sandboxed runner** (F-06 execs `python -m factory.session` under srt), where the real progress log — outside the run dir — is denied, so the tool cannot be a log writer. Instead: `VentCollector(run_id, task_id, seat)` is an in-memory sink (`.turn` incremented by `run_session` as `AssistantMessage`s stream past, one asyncio loop with the SDK's in-process handler calls — turn stamping is best effort and documented as such); `make_vent_tool`/`vent_server` build the `vent(category, text)` SDK MCP tool; `SessionRecord` gains `vents: list` (default empty — every pre-F-27 record still parses) and **every exit path** of `run_session` carries the collected vents out (`_carry`), including aborts and SDK failures: friction recorded in a run that failed is exactly the friction worth having. The orchestrator — outside, unconfined — passes `run_id`/`task_id` to both seat calls (the reviewer sits in the clean gate clone, but its vents belong to the task's run; both ride `work.run_id`), and `flush_vents` appends **one progress-log entry per vent** the moment each session returns, before any gate can crash — `{kind: "vent", run_id, task_id, seat, turn, category, text, at}`, hash-chained like every other entry. Categories are the WO's five, enforced twice (JSON-Schema enum the SDK validates before the handler runs, + a handler raise the SDK wraps into a model-readable isError — a malformed call costs a retry, never a session); text is capped at 2000 chars (truncation marker, never a rejection — a too-long complaint still records) and goes through `redact_credentials` before storage (F-25's invariant: untrusted session text reaching orchestrator-owned storage is redacted; `factory status` later prints these). Attempt records are untouched — the schema's `additionalProperties: false` on attempts means AC-3 holds by construction, not by care. `weeks_vents(factory_root, project, now=...)` is the single reading seam: trailing 7-day window, all five category keys always present (empty groups included — a status page renders a stable shape), non-vent entries ignored, a malformed `at` keeps the vent (a bad stamp must not hide friction from the owner), strictly read-only. No schema change (no new statuses/escalations), no `prompts/` change (the CLI advertises MCP tools to the model itself, so no PROMPT_PINS bump), no `tasks.json` touch.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f27.py::test_ac_1` — for **both seats**, a session's built options list `vent` in `allowed_tools` and carry the in-process MCP server (`mcp_servers["vent"]["type"] == "sdk"`); the tool is named `vent`, its JSON Schema carries exactly the five categories, every category records (handler answers success, never isError), an outside-enum category raises; and a call made **mid-stream** (the fake query awaits the handler between messages, as the real SDK would) is stamped with the turn it happened in and rides the record out (`record.vents`).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — the handler caps text (`≤ VENT_TEXT_CAP`, shorter than sent) and redacts credentials before storing; a before/after tree-hash around handler calls proves the tool writes **nothing else** (it has no file code — the test keeps it that way); `flush_vents` appends exactly one entry per vent with every AC-2 field plus the `at` stamp; end to end (real fixture repo through the real sandbox, stub seats venting): both seats' vents land in `ProgressLog.for_task(root, project, task).jsonl` — the canonical location **outside every clone** — in call order, against the run's own id, after the orchestrator handed each session that `run_id`/`task_id` (asserted via the seat stub's captured kwargs). Vents never render into fresh-session prompts (`render()` skips `kind: "vent"`; the round entry still renders). Extra: `test_sandboxed_spec_carries_run_and_task_ids` — the ids travel in the runner spec, so the in-sandbox collector can stamp them (the record's stdout channel is the only way out).
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — session level: a stream that vents mid-run produces a record identical to the same stream without the vent, stripped only of `vents` and `duration_ms` (the vent list; measured wall time); orchestrator level: two full runs (quiet vs both seats venting) — outcomes equal (`pr_opened`), attempts identical modulo the run's own identity (run_id/started/ended/validator_report), budget numbers (turns, input/output tokens) equal, and vents present only in the venting run's progress log, never in `tasks.json`.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — `weeks_vents` groups the trailing seven days by category across the project's task logs: in-window vents from two tasks listed with task/run/seat/turn/at context, a 13-day-old vent excluded, a non-vent round entry ignored, every category key present; a before/after tree-hash proves it is read-only; and the docstring pins its two named consumers (`factory status`/F-11 lists, F-19 reads) so F-11's session wires this seam, not a second implementation. `factory status` itself is F-11 (unbuilt, later in the build order) — this WO's deliverable is the seam it will call, exactly as the AC's forward reference reads.
- **Extra coverage:** `test_build_options_without_a_collector_has_no_vent_server` — `build_options` called directly (the f06 option-shape tests' cut) still builds: the grant-list entry alone attaches nothing.
- **Also changed:** `progress.py` (`safe_name` public — F-27's reader walks the same layout `for_task` builds; module docstring documents vent entries; `render()` excludes them with the why); `session.py` docstring's seat list documents the tool and its never-touches-the-budget rule; `orchestrator.py` docstring bullet; VALIDATOR-SPEC's rounds paragraph gains the vent sentence (protected path, declared by F-27's `factory_control` risk flag).
- **Evidence:** 6 F-27 tests / **498 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` on the branch head `5095308` (scratch clone, base `c3fbb54`, node_modules-padded via `paths.setup_outputs`, unconfined runner — the F-26 arrangement): verdict **pass** — all four contract refs red on base (`red_reason: collection`), green on head, no findings.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-27 owner review round (PR #42, head `9082b0a`; 10 findings — all fixed, 8 red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review8`, finding 9 closed by those tests, finding 10 a ledger/PR-body correction)

All eight review tests were red against `9082b0a` before any fix, each on the defect itself (review1: `flush_vents` had no stamping seam and passed forged keys through; review2: a straddling `ghp_` key survived truncation; review3: turn `3` stamped where the vent was made in turn `2`; review4: no `VENT_QUALIFIED_NAME`, the grant was the bare `vent`; review5: a raising flush ended the run `orchestrator_error`; review6: `weeks_vents` raised on one corrupt log; review7: vents carried no call-time `at`; review8: five vents meant five full log reads).

- **(1) Vents crossed the sandbox boundary unvalidated** (correctness/security): `flush_vents` rebuilt nothing — `{"kind": "vent", **vent}` let a record override `kind`, inject `finding_keys` (seeded into F-23 convergence by the orchestrator's log scan) or carry `n`/`prev` (crashing `append`). It now rebuilds every entry from the whitelisted fields (`kind` fixed to `vent`), drops non-mappings / out-of-enum categories / non-string text, re-redacts and re-caps the text, coerces `turn` to a non-negative int, and takes `run_id`/`task_id`/`seat` from the orchestrator (`_log_vents` passes what it actually ran). `test_review1`: a forged-key vent, a bad category, a non-dict, a leaking key and a non-string text → two clean entries; end to end a forged `kind: "round"` vent never seeds `finding_keys` nor renders.
- **(2) Truncate-then-redact** (security): new `clean_text` redacts first, then caps. `test_review2`.
- **(3) `turn` counted content blocks and could lag the handler** (correctness): `VentCollector.observe` counts distinct `message_id`s on main-thread assistant messages (subagent messages don't advance the turn), records each `mcp__vent__vent` `tool_use` block's input, and re-stamps the matching vent (by category + raw text, in order) with its block's turn; a schema-rejected call has a block but no vent and matches nothing; a session cut off before the block streamed keeps the call-time turn. `test_review3`: three blocks in turn 1 (one a rejected call), the handler firing before turn 2's block is consumed → stamped `2`.
- **(4) The grant named nothing** (correctness): the CLI exposes SDK MCP tools as `mcp__<server>__<tool>` (confirmed in the pinned bundled CLI); both seats now grant `VENT_QUALIFIED_NAME` = `mcp__vent__vent`. `test_review4` pins the name and that it is exactly `mcp__{server}__{tool}` for the built options; `test_ac_1` and the no-collector test assert the qualified name.
- **(5) A failed flush crashed the run** (correctness, AC-3): the orchestrator's `_log_vents` wraps the flush — a failure is a stderr line, never an `orchestrator_error` or a lost attempt. `test_review5` (flush monkeypatched to raise → `pr_opened`, one attempt, the error on stderr). Not changed: a log whose chain is already broken still fails the round's own `finish()` append exactly as before F-27 — that is the log's integrity rule, not vent telemetry.
- **(6) One corrupt log hid every task's vents** (correctness): `weeks_vents` skips a log that fails to parse/verify and appends its path to an optional `unreadable` list for the status page. `test_review6`.
- **(7) All vents carried the flush time** (correctness): the collector stamps `at` per call; `flush_vents`' `at` is now the fallback for a missing/unparsable stamp and the ceiling for a future one (a vent cannot be dated forward to linger in the window). `test_review7`; `test_ac_1`/`test_ac_2` assert the call-time stamp.
- **(8) O(m·n) flush** (efficiency): new `ProgressLog.append_many` — one lock, one read-and-verify, one fsync, chained identically to m appends (`append` is now `append_many([entry])[0]`); the n/prev guard runs over the whole batch before anything is written. `test_review8` counts one `_read` for five vents and checks the chain.
- **(9) The tests could not catch 3 and 4** (test-coverage): closed by `test_review3` (multi-block turns with real `message_id`s and `ToolUseBlock`s, handler-before-block ordering) and `test_review4` (the CLI-facing name).
- **(10) The ledger's AC-1 evidence claimed the grant was `vent`** (docs): the F-27 entry above stands as the record of what was built at `9082b0a`; this round supersedes its AC-1 line — the grant is `mcp__vent__vent` (the tool's own `name` stays `vent`; the CLI qualifies it). The PR body's AC-1 bullet is corrected to match.
- **Evidence (review round):** 8 review tests red against `9082b0a`; after the fixes 14 F-27 tests / **506 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Dogfood `run_l1` was **not** re-run this round.

### F-27 reconcile review round (PR #44; 5 findings — queue re-scope + test-environment hardening; owner-directed)

- **(1) The frozen contract asserted unbuilt consumers** (correctness): AC-4 as reconciled read "`factory status` (F-11) lists the week's vents grouped by category, and F-19's review reads them" — observably false with `factory status` still a stub (`cli.STUBS` maps it to F-11) and F-19 unbuilt, and the frozen `acceptance_hash` would turn any later rewording into F-03's `flagged_done` flag instead of a normal re-approval. Fixed with the owner directing this round: AC-4 is re-scoped to what F-27 shipped — the read-only `weeks_vents` seam whose docstring names its two consumers — and the contract is re-frozen over the new acceptance list with the project's own `factory.contract.acceptance_hash` (`approved_at` bumped to 2026-10-23T11:33:50Z, `approved_by` unchanged: Anthony). The F-27 entries' AC-4 evidence lines above stand — `test_ac_4` proves exactly the re-scoped criterion; only the contract's wording changed, which this round supersedes.
- **(3) The vent-listing obligation was orphaned the moment F-27 went done** (correctness): neither F-11's nor F-19's contract mentioned vents, so a future F-11 session could ship `factory status` against its own three criteria without ever calling `weeks_vents`, and no gate would catch the omission — vents recorded forever, surfaced nowhere. The obligation moved onto the tasks that build it: F-11 gains an AC (`factory status` lists the week's vents from the seam, no second implementation) and F-19 gains one (the weekly review reads the seam alongside failures and transcripts). BUILD-ORDER.md mirrors all three texts so a future import proposes no diff.
- **(4) The reconcile session's local suite ran another branch's code** (test evidence): `pytest -q` in this worktree imported `factory` from the shared editable install, which points at the main checkout (then on `feat/f30-run-trace`), so the 505 "passing" tests exercised F-30 work-in-progress and the one failure was the checkout mismatch, not this branch. Reproduced on this branch: `srt_bin()` resolved under `Software_Factory_Agents` while the test's `REPO_ROOT` was the worktree. Fix: a new `tests/conftest.py` guard aborts any session whose `factory` import is not under the repo under test, with the remedy in the message (run `python -m pytest` or `PYTHONPATH=.`, or give the checkout its own environment) — a mechanical rule as a test, per CLAUDE.md. CI installs the branch under test into a fresh job, so the guard is green there by construction.
- **(5) The srt_bin test conflated the override check with the module path** (test coverage): `test_srt_bin_ignores_env_overrides_entirely` asserted equality against the test file's own repo root, so it failed in any second worktree even with the env override correctly ignored — reading as a sandbox regression and inviting a commit past it. It now asserts the env var is ignored (`!= /tmp/definitely-not-srt`) and anchors the expected path to the imported package itself; the now-unused `REPO_ROOT` constant is gone from `test_f05.py`.
- **(2) Committed over a red local suite** (conventions): the session committed `08550bc` immediately after `1 failed, 505 passed`, against CLAUDE.md's "`ruff check .` and `pytest -q` must pass before any commit". The red was finding 4's environmental mismatch — but the rule exists precisely so a red is investigated, never assumed environmental. No remedy beyond (4) and (5), which make this specific misleading red impossible; this entry is the acknowledgment, and the rule stands.
- **Evidence (this round):** the wrong-checkout condition reproduced red pre-fix in this worktree (`test_srt_bin_ignores_env_overrides_entirely` failing with `srt_bin()` under the main checkout); post-fix, the same bare run aborts at the conftest guard naming the shadowed checkout, and `python -m pytest -q` from this worktree (cwd first on `sys.path`, guard confirming `factory.__file__` under `sfa-f27-reconcile`) runs **506 tests green** against this branch's code with this branch's own `npm ci`-installed srt 0.0.79, ruff clean. The worktree first had to get its own `npm ci`: without it 134 sandbox tests fail for lack of the pinned srt — the same incomplete-worktree class as finding 4, and proof the reviewed session's "505 passing" ran the main checkout's code *and* its node_modules. CI remains the authoritative run for #44. Dogfood `run_l1` was **not** re-run (docs + test-infrastructure round).

## F-30 — Run trace

- **Commits:** `feat/f30-run-trace` → PR (this one). Red-first: the suite was red at collection on the missing `factory.trace` module (ImportError — the F-26/F-27 pattern), and the dogfood run below proves all contract refs red on base (`red_reason: collection`): the test file applied to base imports the not-yet-existing module.
- **Built:** 2026-10-23. Interactive session after the full re-read (BUILD-ORDER §F-30 + the queue entry, TASK-CONTRACT, VALIDATOR-SPEC, the F-25/F-26/F-27 ledger entries incl. every review round, `session.py` with the F-27 vent-tap precedent, `sandbox.py`'s select pump, `orchestrator.py` `_run_task` end to end, `runs.py`, `cli.py`, `sentinels.redact_credentials`/`CREDENTIAL_PATTERNS` — the docstring already earmarked them for F-30, `l0/l1/l2` report shapes, the f05/f06/f10/f27 test harnesses, the SDK's UserMessage/ToolResultBlock constructors); branched from `origin/main` `fce07e6` (#43 merged; F-30..F-33 already in `tasks.json`). Local checkout first refreshed from the stale `feat/f27-vent-tool` (main had moved past it by #42/#43).
- **Design notes:** new `factory/trace.py`. The one structural fact the WO forces: AC-1 demands the trace live **outside the sandbox's write allowlist**, so the session runner — inside srt — cannot write `tool_call` events to the file. Split by trust: the orchestrator (outside, unconfined) owns the `TraceWriter`; the runner **taps its SDK stream** (`_tap_tool_calls`: a `tool_use` block opens a pending call, its `tool_result` closes it as one event — name, args/result snippets, ok, started/ended) and ships events out as `factory-trace `-prefixed stdout lines, flushed as they happen; `run_sandboxed` grew `on_stdout_line` (complete lines handed to the callback from the pump loop, before the child exits; captured output unchanged); the orchestrator-side `trace_line_forwarder` scrubs the seat-token literal off the raw line before parsing and appends through the writer; the record stays the last un-prefixed line. `TraceWriter` is the single write path: stamps seq/ts/run/task/attempt, redacts every string (literal secrets ≥ 8 chars — a shorter literal redacts noise, not secrets — plus F-25's `redact_credentials`, still the only pattern list), caps (2000 for tool snippets, 4000 for any string), and fails once: a failing write is reported once on stderr and the writer disables itself — AC-5's never-changes-a-run, held structurally (no trace call site can raise). Phases are context managers (`setup`/`implementer`/`L0`/`L1`/`L2`/`publish`): a raise still writes `phase_end` (marked `error`) so no phase dangles; session phases collect their seat's `SessionRecord` into the phase_end token totals — AC-7's no-second-source: the same records `attempt_record` sums. `gate_checks(report)` derives the AC-3 rows: per L0 command (command line + output tail), per sentinel result, one `diff_policy` row; per L1 red/green proof (ref, which tree, exit code, red reason); per L2 finding plus a `verdict` row. `run_end` is the terminal marker `--follow` stops on — **one per run** (idempotent; a failed round that continues to the next attempt is not terminal — `finish()` marks only when the task transitions), stamped on every outcome (pass → `published`, fail/escalated → `failed`, aborted/peak/quota → `aborted`) with an error-path safety net marking a crashed run. `factory trace <run-id> [--follow]` (a real subcommand now): the run id is pattern-checked (`RUN_ID_RE`, moved to `runs.py` so the reclaim regex and the reader share the one shape), the project comes from the profile next to the queue, and reading is read-only — follow pushes back a line caught mid-write and stops at `run_end`. No schema change (traces are not `tasks.json`), no `paths.protected` change (F-28's owner step adds `factory/trace.py` there — not this WO's to touch).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f30.py::test_ac_1` — after a passing stub run, `<root>/trace/fixture/<run-id>.jsonl` exists (the attempt's run id), one JSON event per line, every event stamped seq 1..N / run / task / attempt / ts, the six phases present as paired start/end in order, two `session_end` rows (implementer, reviewer) each with tokens-by-component / turns / model / cost, `gate_check` rows with layer/item/ok/note, and the last line `run_end` `published`; plus a real srt session (`build_settings` + `run_sandboxed`) **cannot write the trace path** — the redirect fails, no file appears.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — a fake SDK stream under `run_session(trace_sink=writer.append)`: the `tool_call` line (with its result) is asserted on disk **inside the generator, before it yields the ResultMessage**.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — pass run: every layer's rows ok with substantive notes (L0: the setup and test command rows, the sentinel rows, `diff_policy`; L1: exactly `red:`/`green:` for the contract ref, each note naming the ref; L2: the verdict row); fail runs per layer: L0's failing test-command row ok=false; L1's `red:<ref>` row ok=false (a test already green on base proves nothing); L2's finding row ok=false carrying the reviewer's text.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a fake tool call whose args echo the seat token (deliberately not shaped like any F-25 pattern, so only the literal scrub catches it) and whose result also carries a `ghp_…` key: neither appears anywhere in the file, `[redacted]` does; the 5000-char args value is capped to 2000 with a marker, the result snippet bounded, every line bounded.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `<root>/trace/fixture` pre-created mode 0o555 (every write raises): the run still reaches `pr_opened`, the attempt record is identical to an untraced run's modulo the run's identity (run_id/started/ended/validator_report), and exactly one stderr report names the failure.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — `factory trace <run-id>` prints the file's lines in order, one per event; a run id with no trace (and a non-run-id argument) exit 1 on stderr; `--follow` keeps waiting for appended events and exits on the terminal marker (a mid-follow append simulated through a sleep shim — it really waited); a before/after comparison proves reading changed nothing (bytes and directory listing untouched).
- **AC-7** proven: `test_ac_7` — the pass run's `phase_end` token rows (implementer + L2, the two session phases) sum exactly to the attempt's `input_tokens`/`output_tokens` in `tasks.json` (180/50) — derived from the same records, no second spend ledger.
- **Extra coverage:** `test_open_tool_calls_are_traced_when_the_session_dies` — a stream that raises mid-call still traces its open calls (started, result None, ok None); the flush rides the close-stream `finally`, so aborted sessions keep their evidence. `test_one_terminal_marker_per_run` — three failed rounds → exactly one `run_end`, the run's last line, attempts 2 and 3 still stamped on their own events (failed-but-continuing rounds are not terminal). `test_sandboxed_stdout_events_cross_the_pipe` — the transport halves: the forwarder scrubs the token literal, ignores the record line and garbage; the stdout sink prints exactly one prefixed, flushed line. `test_run_sandboxed_streams_lines_to_the_callback` — a fake pinned srt: complete lines reach `on_stdout_line` in order while the captured output is unchanged.
- **Also changed:** `factory/runs.py` (`RUN_ID_RE` — the run-id shape has one home; the orchestrator's reclaim regex and the CLI's trace reader share it); `factory/session.py` (the tap, the transport pair, `trace_sink` on `run_session`/`run_session_sandboxed`/`_run_prepared`; record parsing takes the last un-prefixed stdout line); `factory/sandbox.py` (`on_stdout_line`); `factory/cli.py` (the trace subcommand; the module docstring now lists it among the real commands); `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` (the rounds paragraph gains the trace sentence — a protected path, declared by this WO's `factory_control` risk flag).
- **Evidence:** 11 F-30 tests / **517 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean. Dogfood red on base `fce07e6`: the F-30 test file applied to a scratch clone of `main` fails all 11 at collection (`ImportError: cannot import name 'trace'` — `red_reason: collection`); green on the branch head `b80ef35` (the suite above).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-30 owner review round (PR #45; 8 findings — all fixed under `test_review1`–`test_review6`)

Five of the six review tests were red against `87c78b9` (scratch worktree at `/tmp/sfa-red`, the editable-install finder stripped from `sys.meta_path` so `factory` resolved to the worktree copy — review5's red ImportError names the worktree path, proving the isolation). The sixth (`test_review6`, the pump's quadratic copy) is an efficiency-only change with no observable behaviour difference — green on arrival, kept as the split-line regression guard (F-25's review10 precedent). Two defects in the fixes themselves were caught before commit: the first cut chmod'ed the trace dir to 0700 unconditionally, which silently REPAIRED `test_ac_5`'s read-only sabotage (the writer now tightens only a directory it created itself — an existing dir keeps its mode: never widened, never "fixed"); and review3's third sub-case forgot to reset its sleep counter, so the deletion never fired and the follow — correctly, by its documented no-`live` contract — waited forever (found by isolating the test after the suite stalled).

- **(1) Untrusted sandbox events could override the stamps and forge a terminal marker** (security): `trace_line_forwarder` accepts only `type == "tool_call"` — the one type the session-side tap emits; anything else printed on that pipe (reachable from inside the sandbox) is dropped. Defence in depth: `TraceWriter.append` merges its stamps AFTER the event (`{**event, seq, ts, …}`), so no event dict can override seq/ts/run/task/attempt even if a new forwarding path appears later. `test_review1`: a forged `run_end`/`session_end` never crosses; a forged `tool_call` crosses but is re-stamped by the writer.
- **(2) `--follow` hung forever when no run_end could come** (correctness): `read_trace(follow=True, live=…)` — `live` says whether the run might still write (the CLI passes run-dir-exists). Once false, `GONE_POLLS` (25 × 0.2 s) grace polls for stragglers, then the reader stops and says why on stderr; a trace file deleted under the reader (retention) stops it too. Without `live`, follow waits for `run_end` however long — the caller opts into the bound. Known edge, recorded: a SIGKILLed run's dir survives until the next reclaim, so its follow stops only then (or at the operator's Ctrl-C — the CLI exits 130). `test_review3` covers all three stops plus the live-reset.
- **(3) A plain read stopped at the first run_end** (correctness): `read_trace` returns on `run_end` only in follow mode; non-follow prints the whole file — cleanup-time events after a marker (and anything a forged marker tried to hide) are no longer truncated. `test_review2`.
- **(4) Traces stored repo content forever, world-readable** (privacy): the writer creates its directory 0700 (only when it creates it — see the ac_5 regression above) and chmods the file 0600 on first write; `prune_traces` (run by `reclaim_runs`, under the lease that says no run is appending) deletes run-id-shaped `.jsonl` files older than `RETENTION_DAYS` (14). The PII exposure is documented in VALIDATOR-SPEC's trace paragraph. `test_review5`: modes pinned on a real run; age-out directly and through `reclaim_runs`; fresh files stay.
- **(5) TraceWriter was built without the seat token** (security): `_run_task` passes `extra_secrets=[seat_token() or ""]` — orchestrator-written events (`session_end` reasons, gate notes carrying command-output tails) now get the literal-token scrub the session-side tap already had, closing the gap where the token appears in an error string no F-25 shape matches. No dedicated test (the wiring is one constructor argument; the scrub itself is AC-4's writer-side `_clean`).
- **(6) KeyboardInterrupt traced as failed** (correctness): the outer `except BaseException` maps `KeyboardInterrupt`/`SystemExit` to `aborted`, keeping `failed` for real faults — F-11's interventions and F-19's failure grouping read `failed` as "the factory or the agent broke". `test_review4`: a Ctrl-C mid-run leaves `run_end aborted` as the trace's last line.
- **(7) Snippet redact+cap duplicated across modules** (reuse): `trace.snippet(text, secret, cap=SNIPPET_CAP)` is the one implementation; `session._trace_snippet` and its private `_TRACE_TRUNCATED` copy are gone (the F-27 review's finding-2 order — redact, then cap — now has exactly one home). The AC-4 tests exercise it from both sides.
- **(8) The pump copied the whole partial every chunk** (efficiency): `_pump` searches only the new chunk (`chunk.rfind(b"\n")`); the accumulated partial is copied once, when a line is actually emitted — a multi-MB newline-free line over thousands of reads no longer costs quadratic copying inside the select loop (which also delayed the timeout check). `test_review6` pins the split-line delivery the rewrite must preserve.
- **Also changed:** `factory/cli.py` (`--follow` passes `live`; help text updated); VALIDATOR-SPEC's trace paragraph (trust boundary, follow-stop, privacy/retention); trace.py module docstring.
- **Evidence (review round):** 5 review tests red against `87c78b9` (review6 green-as-guard); after the fixes 17 F-30 tests / **523 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean.

### F-30 owner review round 2 (PR #45; 1 finding — fixed under `test_review7`)

- **(2, residual) `--follow` still hung on a killed run** (correctness): round 1's live signal was "the run dir exists", but a SIGKILLed orchestrator leaves its run dir on disk until the next `factory loop` reclaims it, so the headline case of finding (2) still followed forever. The live signal is now the run lease: new `state.run_lease_held(queue_path)` opens `<queue>.run.lock` read-only (never creating it, never writing the holder line) and tries a non-blocking shared `flock`; refused means an exclusive holder is alive, and the OS drops that lock the moment the holder dies. The CLI's `live` is `run_dir.exists() and run_lease_held(queue)`. The lease is per queue, so a later run of the same project holding it keeps an old follow waiting until that run ends: bounded, never forever. Known cost, documented on the function: during the probe's microsecond shared lock, a racing `begin_run` would see the lease busy and refuse with its normal, retryable `RunInProgressError`.
- **Red-first:** `tests/test_f30.py::test_review7_follow_stops_on_a_killed_run_and_waits_on_a_held_lease` failed against `d833a2b` on its hang guard ("--follow is still polling a killed run"). After the fix it covers: the killed-run case stops within the grace window; a held lease (another open file description) keeps it following until released; the probe leaves the holder line untouched and doesn't create a missing lock file.
- **Real-process check:** a child process holding the lease → `run_lease_held` True; after `kill -9` → False, holder line intact.
- **Evidence (round 2):** 18 F-30 tests / **524 total** passing locally (`factory` imported from this checkout, macOS, real srt; stub seats — no model spend), ruff clean.

## F-33 — Sentinel false-positive corpus

- **Commits:** `feat/f33-sentinel-corpus` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f33.py` landed first (commit `c1b95a7`) — 13 tests red against the pre-F-33 tree (10 on the missing artifacts: 0-patch benign corpus, empty attack corpus, `invalid choice: 'sentinels'` from argparse, `corpus/**` absent from `paths.protected`, `ImportError` on `changes_from_patch`; 3 on the F-25 scanner semantics: moved/edited assertions and string-fixture markers flagged, env reads flagged as secrets) with the rest of the suite green (10 failed / 525 passed, ruff clean) — plus the focused guards that pin behaviour F-25 already had (statement-level markers still flag, real comment-syntax suppressions still flag, an env fallback literal still flags, net assertion loss flags, commented-out assertions flag) green on arrival in the F-25 review6 style.
- **Built:** 2026-10-23. Interactive session after the full re-read (BUILD-ORDER §F-33 + the queue entry, VALIDATOR-SPEC L0 §4, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/gates.py`'s diff machinery, the F-25 ledger incl. both review rounds, `cli.py`'s trace precedent, the F-30 read-only-test pattern). Branched from `origin/main` `ec3965c` (#46 merged; F-33's only dependency F-25 is done).
- **Design notes:** the corpus is the repo's own history: 45 merged PRs fetched as `gh pr diff` (`corpus/benign/<n>.patch`), scanned with the **pre-F-33** sentinels against the real profile's risk globs before any fix — 46 findings across four classes, each triaged against the actual diff lines. Three PRs are excluded as **correct hits** (#19: a fixture secret file proving redaction; #37: the sentinel tests themselves — seeded credential fixtures, skip fixtures, lint prose, 30 hits; #42: credential-shaped vent text proving vents redact); 42 remain. The other four classes were false positives and drove four red-first sentinel fixes, each fix shaped by what the real diffs showed, not by the corpus's shape: `assertion_removed` became per-file net arithmetic (verbatim re-adds pair; a file ending with as many assertion lines as it started is quiet; comments are neither assertions nor replacements — PRs #2/#11/#27/#28/#35/#44 had 11 hits); `skip_marker_added` anchored to statement level (PR #8's scanner tests embed `@pytest.mark.skip` inside quoted patch fixtures); `lint_suppression_added` requires a comment syntax the file's language carries (`# noqa` inside JSON/markdown prose quotes the pattern — PRs #25/#37 documenting the sentinels); `credential_added`'s generic "secret assignment" shape yields on environment reads (`token = os.environ.get("ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN", "")` is the safe pattern — PR #18) while the prefix shapes (AKIA/ghp_/sk_live…) still scan env-read lines, so a hardcoded fallback still fires. `redact_credentials` and `CREDENTIAL_PATTERNS` are untouched — trace (F-30) and vent (F-27) redaction is unchanged. New substrate: `gates.changes_from_patch` (statuses from the git headers, line detail through `_parse_unified_diff`, so replay and `diff_file_changes` share one hunk parser); new `factory/replay.py` (`patch_findings`, corpus resolution, one-line-per-sentinel-per-diff formatting with counts, never lines); `factory sentinels replay [--corpus benign|attack] [<patch> ...]` in the CLI (profile read from next to `--queue`, the trace pattern; misuse/missing corpus exit 1, never a traceback). The attack corpus (`corpus/attack/<sentinel>-<n>.patch`, name = the sentinel that must flag it) is 10 hand-written diffs written fresh — separate files from the F-25 AC-3 seeds, which stay inline literals in `tests/test_f25.py`; `test_ac_2` pins that separation structurally (test_f25.py must not grow a corpus path).
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f33.py::test_ac_1` — 42 benign patches (≥ 20), every file named by PR number, zero findings across the corpus against this repo's real profile (loaded through `load_profile`, so the gate holds against production config). The `factory sentinels replay --corpus benign` run over the real repo prints 168/168 pass lines (42 × 4 sentinels).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — 10 attack patches, every built-in covered (`credential_added` ×3, `skip_marker_added` ×3, `assertion_removed` ×2, `lint_suppression_added` ×2), each file flagged by its named sentinel through the real replay engine (`factory.replay.patch_findings`), and the F-25 separation pinned.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — the CI gate: asserts `ci.yml` still runs pytest on `pull_request` (CI's `pytest -q` is what executes this test on every PR), then replays every built-in over both corpora; the three failure modes each name the sentinel and the corpus file: a benign hit (`{f.id} fired on corpus/benign/{file}`), a sentinel with no attack diff, a missed attack (`{sentinel} does not flag corpus/attack/{file}`).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — the real CLI over a fixture corpus: `--corpus benign` prints exactly one line per sentinel per diff (4 lines, one patch), every sentinel `pass`; explicit patch selection reports the fixture lint attack as `fail`; the no-argument form covers both corpora (8 lines); before/after sha256+mtime snapshots of a populated factory root AND the corpus repo are identical after all three runs (reads only). `test_replay_refuses_misuse` — `--corpus` + patches together, a valid profile with no corpus, and a missing patch file each exit 1 with a message.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `corpus/**` is in `paths.protected` of this repo's `.factory/project.yaml` (this PR), and `matches()` covers `corpus/README.md`, benign and attack patches.
- **Extra coverage:** `changes_from_patch` parses A/M/D/R statuses, old paths and hunk line numbers; moved/edited assertions stay quiet; a verbatim re-add is a move even when the file shrinks net; net assertion loss flags every unmatched line; commenting out an assertion is a removal; marker text in string fixtures stays quiet while ten statement-level marker forms still flag; quoted suppressions in JSON/markdown stay quiet while hash/html/slash-comment suppressions still flag; an env read is not a committed secret while an env fallback literal still fires (and its finding never echoes the token).
- **Also changed:** `factory/sentinels.py` (the four scanner fixes + module docstring); `factory/gates.py` (`changes_from_patch`); `factory/cli.py` (the `sentinels replay` subcommand); `.factory/project.yaml` (`corpus/**` under `paths.protected`); `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` (the L0 built-ins sentence now carries the tightened semantics; the Validating-the-validator section gains the corpus paragraph — a protected path, covered by this WO's `factory_control` risk flag); `corpus/` (42 benign + 10 attack patches + README with the exclusion ledger).
- **Evidence:** 18 F-33 tests (5 AC + 13 focused/guard) / full suite below passing locally (macOS; `factory` import-identity confirmed before every run), ruff clean. Red: 13 failed / 525 passed on the pre-implementation tree (see Commits); the same corpus scanned pre-fix showed 46 findings across 45 merged PRs — the triage table is the work order's substance and lives in `corpus/README.md`.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` once this PR's CI is green (ruff + pytest on 3.11 + 3.13) and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-33 owner review round (PR #47; 7 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review7`)

Four review tests were red against `78fc9ee` (the review's own probes, reproduced: every silent cheat named below was flagged on `main`); two were green-on-arrival guards in the F-30 review6 style (`test_review4` honest consolidations, `test_review7` the catch-all lint pin — the syntax filter already held); `test_review2`'s guard role is carried by the existing `test_marker_text_in_string_fixtures_does_not_trip`, which stayed green through the fix. Six corpus-gate failures rode along red (`test_ac_2`/`test_ac_3` on the new boundary attack files).

- **(1) Line-start anchoring un-flagged real skips** (correctness): `@unittest.skipIf` (`\b` fails before `If`), `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skip(...)` (a whole module skipped), `if cond: pytest.skip(...)`, `pytest.param(..., marks=pytest.mark.xfail)` all passed on the PR head — the exact cheating path F-25 exists to block. Fix: matching now runs on the line with quoted spans blanked (`_outside_strings`) instead of anchoring to line start, and `@unittest\.skip` lost its `\b` (covers skipIf/skipUnless): every statement shape fires, fixture strings stay inert. `test_review1`.
- **(2) Count parity hid tautologies and padding** (correctness): `assert answer() == 42` → `assert True`, or → any line whose STRING merely contains "assert", levelled the count and passed. Fix: a replacement must be a real assertion statement (`_is_replacement_assertion`: `^assert\b`, `receiver.assert*(`, `expect(` — matched on the string-blanked line; `assert True`/constants excluded). The removed side stays deliberately broad, so an honest consolidation still escalates: consequence handled under (5)'s sibling decision — PR #2 leaves the corpus as a correct hit (below). `test_review3` (red) + `test_review4` (green guard: 1-for-1 edits and 2-for-2 rewrites stay quiet).
- **(3) Env-call text anywhere exempted a secret** (security): a trailing `# os.getenv("KEY")` comment, an `or "literal"` fallback, and a fallback argument all exempted the secret-assignment shape — and that sentinel escalates with no retry, so the hole bypassed the strongest guard. Fix: the exemption blanks only the call head and its first argument (`_ENV_CALL_SPAN`); the shape then tests the remainder, so a 16+ literal anywhere else on the line flags. `test_review5` (lines 1–3 red, 4–5 the PR #18 shape stays clean).
- **(4) The attack corpus didn't probe the relaxed boundaries** (test coverage): six new attack files, one per moved boundary — `skip_marker_added-4` (skipIf/pytestmark/inline/param), `assertion_removed-3` (`assert True` swap), `assertion_removed-4` (string padding), `credential_added-4` (env-comment), `credential_added-5` (`or`-fallback), `credential_added-6` (secret behind a quoted path, which also exercises finding 7 end to end). The gate (`test_ac_3`) rode red on all of them before the fixes; now 16 attacks, every one flagged by its named sentinel (the `factory sentinels replay` run: 16 fail rows, zero benign fails).
- **(5) Lint benign evidence covered only control files** (test coverage): under this repo's risk globs almost no Python is in lint scope, so the "0 benign hits" claimed evidence for JSON/MD only. Resolved both ways the reviewer offered: the corpus is also replayed under a catch-all glob — `test_review7` pins the exact result: six genuine `# noqa` additions in Python files (PR #18 session.py, #22 test_f10 ×2, #40 test_f26 ×2, #45 trace.py — correct hits had those files been risk paths) and zero prose/JSON hits — and `corpus/README.md` states the limit explicitly: Python/TSX risk-path benign evidence first exists when JobFinderOS contributes its own corpus (F-13). Also under this heading: PR #2 left the corpus (correct hit — the F-01 review round consolidated three wiring asserts into two; the count rule flags the net reduction, and a human reviewed exactly that at merge); 41 benign PRs remain.
- **(6) Secret-shaped fixtures kept GitGuardian red** (security): all fixture tokens rewritten low-entropy (`ghp_0000…`, `AKIA0000…`, zero runs, a words-only PEM body) — they still match the sentinel's shapes (the gate proves it every PR) but not scanner entropy checks; `corpus/README.md` documents the policy and the fallback (mark a still-flagged fixture a known false positive in the dashboard, never restore high-entropy bytes). The `test_env_fallback_literal_still_flags` unit fixture already constructs its token at runtime.
- **(7) The patch header regex misread quoted / ` b/` paths** (correctness): git quotes exotic paths (`diff --git "a/t\303\253st.py" "b/..."`) and an unquoted path containing ` b/` split wrongly, so every sentinel silently skipped such files. Fix: paths now come from the authoritative lines, decoded (`_unquote_git_path`, `_diff_path`): `+++ b/x` first, `/dev/null` falls back to a deletion's `--- a/x`, `rename to`/`copy to` for no-hunk blocks, the header split only as a last resort — in both `changes_from_patch` and `_parse_unified_diff`, so `diff_file_changes` (which shares the hunk parser) gets the same fix. `test_review6` (quoted modify/deletion/rename + ` b/` path; the removed assertions in the quoted test files still reach the sentinel through replay). Sweeping the same surface found the sibling: git quotes `--name-status` rows the same way (`M\t"tests/t\\303\\253st.py"`), so `_name_status_rows` decodes too and `_unquote_git_path` accepts both quote styles — `diff_file_changes` merges rows and hunks on the same decoded paths (`test_review6b`, red until the row decode landed; a real-git cross-check drove it: the parser fix alone left the row quoted and the merge empty).
- **Also changed:** `corpus/README.md` (exclusion ledger + evidence-limits section + low-entropy policy + updated fix descriptions); `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` (the L0 built-ins parentheticals match the review-round semantics); `corpus/benign/2.patch` removed (exclusion (5)).
- **Evidence (review round):** 4 review tests red against `78fc9ee` + the 6 corpus-gate failures on the new attacks; after the fixes 24 F-33 tests / 46 with the F-25 suite / full run below passing locally (macOS; `factory` import identity confirmed), ruff clean. Both-corpus replay through the real CLI: 228 lines — 16 fail rows (every attack fires its named sentinel), 212 pass, zero benign hits.

## F-22 — Vetted agent toolkit (skills, plugins, MCP servers)

- **Commits:** `feat/f22-toolkit` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f22.py` landed first (commit `ba4528b`) — all 11 tests red at collection on the missing `factory.toolkit` module (the F-26/F-27 pattern), the rest of the suite green (734 passed on the pre-implementation tree).
- **Built:** 2026-10-30. Interactive session (this WO is `factory_control` and touches protected paths, so it was never a dogfood candidate), after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-22 + the queue entry's byte-exact acceptance, TASK-CONTRACT, VALIDATOR-SPEC §Rounds, `factory/session.py` (setting_sources=[], runner staging, the F-27 vent precedent, _run_prepared), `factory/sandbox.py` (build_settings allow_read/allowWrite), `factory/config.py`, both schemas, the F-33 corpus policy, the f06/f27/f10 test harnesses, and the pinned claude-agent-sdk 0.2.168 — `options.skills` (`Skill(name)` grants, `plugin:skill` qualification), `options.plugins` (`--plugin-dir`, local only), `options.mcp_servers` + `strict_mcp_config`). Branched from `origin/main` `f340bc8` (#75, pytest-xdist) after rebase.
- **Design notes:** new `factory/toolkit.py`. The one structural fact the WO forces: skills must reach the CLI with `setting_sources=[]` held (nothing personal, nothing from the clone), and the SDK has no skill-dirs option — so skills ride ONE local plugin: `stage_toolkit` stages the resolved items into `<settings>-toolkit/` (the F-06 runner-staging pattern: outside the clone, `allow_read`-ed, never writable) with a `factory-toolkit` wrapper manifest holding every skill under `skills/<name>/`; `options.plugins` points at it, `options.skills` enables `factory-toolkit:<name>` one by one. MCP servers attach as `mcp_servers` stdio entries built from the index descriptor (exact command/args/env — no ambient merge), each tool granted individually as `mcp__<name>__<tool>`, `strict_mcp_config=True`; they spawn as children of the in-sandbox CLI, so the F-05 sandbox confines them. The index (`toolkit/index.yaml`, schema `schema/toolkit.schema.json`, validated on every load via contract's new public `validate_against_schema`) is content-addressed: per-file sha256 map + aggregate content_hash; load refuses a missing/extra/tampered vendored file (named), a duplicate name, a non-allowlisted or contradictory licence, an aggregator upstream (mcpmarket / awesome-mcp / the GitHub MCP Registry), a missing report, a report that doesn't name the entry's content hash (the version-bump rule), and MCP descriptors that aren't read-only/credential-free. `factory toolkit vet <path> [--profile P] [--report R]` reads a candidate only (licence identified from the LICENSE grant text, 8 scan classes, no execution, no network, findings never echo a blob or credential) and writes nothing to the index — admission stays a human PR. Profiles gain `toolkit: {skills, plugins, mcp_servers, seats: {implementer|reviewer: …}}` (project.schema.json + `_check_profile_toolkit`: unknown name / wrong kind / widening seat refuses the profile at load, F-02-style); `resolve_toolkit` re-checks MCP domains against the *running* project's allowlists (the index is factory-global, allowlists are per project) and honours seat narrowing (the reviewer gets its read-only subset). Sessions: `build_options(toolkit=…)` (new `_apply_toolkit`), `run_session` stamps every record's `skills` with `name@content_hash` on every exit path (the `_carry` precedent), `run_session_sandboxed`/`_run_prepared` stage + carry the toolkit in the spec, `_main` wires it through; the orchestrator resolves per seat before any session starts and `attempt_record` collects the records' skills (the hardcoded `[]` is gone; the session-less sweep keeps `[]` — nothing loaded, honestly).
- **Verification beyond the tests:** the bundled pinned CLI (2.1.301) accepted the whole mechanism in a live probe — `--plugin-dir <staged>` + `--setting-sources=` + a `Skill(factory-toolkit:demo-skill)` grant booted a session that failed only at the (tokenless, expected) 401 auth call, with no plugin/skill errors — so the option shapes the tests pin are shapes the real binary takes.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f22.py::test_ac_1` — a fixture index with real hashes loads with every field (kind/name/upstream/pin/licence/content_hash/report/dir); seven malformed shapes refuse (missing field, kind enum, licence enum, hash shape, dir escape, pin neither form, report absent); a tampered, deleted or extra vendored file refuses at load **naming the file**; the repo's own `toolkit/index.yaml` (empty in this PR) loads against the committed schema. The test recomputes every hash independently of the module.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — `identify_licence` maps grant text to SPDX; MIT passes, "All rights reserved" and a missing LICENSE refuse with the reason named; the index side refuses an entry whose vendored LICENSE contradicts its declared licence; the code allowlist is exactly the contract's five and the schema enum equals it.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — one committed fixture per class under `toolkit/fixtures/vet/` (7 classes) each flagged under its own class, the clean fixture passes; the encoded-blob candidate is built at runtime and never committed (the test guards the corpus: no 256+ base64 run, no credential prefix, in any committed fixture); the vetter leaves every scanned tree byte-identical (reads only); the real CLI exits 0 on clean and 1 on the injection fixture with the class named on stdout.
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — a clean scan through the CLI leaves `index.yaml` byte-identical (a clean scan never admits); the toolkit group offers exactly `vet` (no admit/add — argparse's own choice list); an entry whose report is missing refuses at load; a content bump under an old report refuses (the report must name the content hash it vetted); an aggregator upstream (mcpmarket.com) refuses, the aggregator named.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `vet_descriptor` refuses a write tool, a send tool, a credential-named env key (probe string built at runtime: `ghp_` + zeros — low-entropy, shape-true) and an off-allowlist domain; the clean read-only descriptor passes; the index refuses a write-tool entry at load; `resolve_toolkit` re-refuses a domain outside the *running* profile's allowlists; the session side: `strict_mcp_config` on, the server spawned from the staged descriptor's exact command/args/env, exactly `mcp__docs__search`/`mcp__docs__fetch_doc` granted (one by one), the staged descriptor JSON present for inspection inside the sandbox.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — a profile naming an unknown skill refuses at load (named); a skill name under `mcp_servers` refuses (kind mismatch); a seat section listing what the project doesn't refuses (seats narrow, never widen); narrowing resolves (reviewer: `docs` only, implementer: both); the repo's real project.schema.json accepts the `toolkit` shape and refuses unknown keys and unknown seats.
- **AC-7** proven: `test_ac_7` — a planted personal skill + MCP server in a fake `$HOME/.claude` are invisible in the built options (nothing personal crosses; `setting_sources == []` pinned) while every listed item is present (skill as `factory-toolkit:demo-skill`, MCP server + vent attached, per-tool grants); the staged copy lives outside the clone (path-asserted, nothing toolkit-shaped in the run dir), its bytes equal the vendored files, the sandbox settings `allowRead` it and no `allowWrite` entry contains it; the sandboxed spec carries `toolkit.staged` + items and the written settings allow exactly that read.
- **AC-8** proven: `test_ac_8` — `run_session` stamps the record's `skills` `name@content-hash` on every exit path; end to end through `run_once` (stub seats mimicking the runner's stamping from the `toolkit` kwarg they were handed): the implementer received the full toolkit, the reviewer its narrowed subset, and the attempt in `tasks.json` records exactly the loaded `name@hash` pair set. **AC-9 pending** — the first JobFinderOS admission is PR 2 (this WO's second half, owner-approved).
- **Also changed:** `factory/contract.py` (public `validate_against_schema`; `load_profile(toolkit_root=…)` + the toolkit names/seats check — import deferred to avoid the cycle); `factory/session.py` (the toolkit paragraph in the module docstring; `_apply_toolkit`; `toolkit=` on build_options/run_session/run_session_sandboxed/_run_prepared; `SessionRecord.skills`; spec carries `toolkit`); `factory/orchestrator.py` (per-seat `resolve_toolkit` before any session; `attempt_record` collects skills; the F-26 stray regex gains `-toolkit`); `factory/runs.py` (`cleanup_run` removes the `-toolkit` sibling); `factory/cli.py` (`toolkit vet`, `ToolkitError` in `_RUN_ERRORS` — a mid-run index drift exits a clean 2, task re-queued; `--report` creates its parent dir); `schema/project.schema.json` (`toolkit` + `$defs`); `schema/toolkit.schema.json` (new); `toolkit/index.yaml` (empty until PR 2) + `toolkit/fixtures/vet/` corpus + README; `.factory/project.yaml` (`toolkit/**`, `factory/toolkit.py`, `tests/test_f22.py` under paths.protected); `tests/test_profile_review.py` (`factory/toolkit.py` in MORE_JUDGE_MODULES, `tests/test_f22.py` in MERGED_UNRECONCILED); VALIDATOR-SPEC §Rounds gains the toolkit sentence (protected path, declared by this WO's `factory_control` flag).
- **Hostile re-read before the PR (own diff, per the production-grade rule):** five defects found and fixed with tests, none caught by the AC tests as first written — (1) `cleanup_run` didn't know the `-toolkit` sibling, so every sandboxed toolkit session would leak a staged copy under `sandbox/` forever; (2) the F-26 reclaim stray regex had the same gap for abandoned runs; (3) the index `report` pattern allowed `..` segments (path traversal out of `toolkit/` — the `files` guard had the lookahead, `report` didn't); (4) a UTF-16-encoded candidate decoded as garbage under `errors="replace"`, so the hidden-character class silently missed zero-width markers re-encoded that way — the scan now decodes strict-UTF-8 → UTF-16 → latin-1 so it always sees real characters; (5) `--report` crashed with a traceback when `toolkit/reports/` didn't exist yet (it can't, before the first admission).
- **Protected paths touched (all):** `.factory/**` (project.yaml protected list, BOOTSTRAP.md this entry), `schema/**` (project.schema.json, new toolkit.schema.json), `factory/session.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/contract.py`, `tests/test_profile_review.py`, `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md`, and the new `toolkit/**` + `factory/toolkit.py` + `tests/test_f22.py` (protected by this same PR).
- **Evidence:** 15 F-22 tests (8 AC + 7 focused) / **751 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend; `-n auto` now that #75 merged; the two extra non-F-22 tests are the new profile-review pins for `factory/toolkit.py` and `tests/test_f22.py`), ruff clean. Red on the pre-implementation tree: collection error on the missing module, 734 passing (commit `ba4528b`).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` — AC-1..AC-8 closed by PR #77, AC-9 by the JobFinderOS admission PR #79, owner-approved. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply.

### F-22 owner review round (PR #77; 4 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review4`, plus the two AC tests the hardening re-scoped)

Finding 2 was proven live before any fix, off-suite (one tiny turn through `run_session` — the f06 in-process seam, NOT test_f06's ac_3/ac_4, which were never run): a stub stdio MCP server advertised a listed `read_doc` and an UNLISTED `write_doc`; on head `1b7e9c8` the model called `write_doc`, nothing denied it (`permission_denials: []`), and the stub executed (`marker_exists_after: true`). Under `bypassPermissions` `allowed_tools` only pre-approves — it restricts nothing. After the fix the same turn shows `permission_denials: [{"tool_name": "mcp__docs__write_doc", …}]`, the model's own reply ("write_doc was blocked …, read_doc succeeded"), and `marker_exists_after: false`.

- **(1) Symlink exfiltration** (security, reproduced by the reviewer): `rglob`+`is_file`+`read_bytes` and `copytree(symlinks=False)` all FOLLOW symlinks — a vendored `link.txt -> ~/.ssh/id_ed25519` would be hashed and staged as a regular file holding the private key, readable by the session (and git stores symlinks, so an admission PR can carry one past a skim). Fix: one walker (`_scan_tree`) underpins everything — it refuses any symlink (file or dir) or non-regular file (fifo/socket/device) by name and recurses only through real directories, so no path can escape the item dir; `file_hashes` hashes regular files only; vet flags a ninth class `symlink` (VET_CLASSES grew — the contract's eight stay in order, the ninth is fail-closed surplus); `_check_entry` refuses at load naming the entry; `stage_toolkit` refuses pre-copy. `test_review1`: a symlinked file, a symlinked dir and a fifo are each refused at vet/load/stage; the link target's content never enters the content hash and never reaches any staged copy (a refused stage leaves nothing behind).
- **(2) MCP tool restriction was ineffective under bypassPermissions** (security): the live reproduction above. Fix: `mcp_guard_hook` (session.py, beside the protected-path and bash guards) — a PreToolUse hook over `mcp__.*` that denies every `mcp__<server>__<tool>` call whose pair the seat's toolkit never granted (unlisted tools of listed servers, any tool of unlisted servers, malformed names), passes the granted pairs (and the in-process vent tool), and never touches built-in tools. Deny hooks still block under `bypassPermissions` (F-06's established fact) — the hook, not the grant list, is what enforces AC-5's "one by one". `test_review2` (red: no mcp hook existed in the built options): unlisted/unknown/malformed denied, listed tools + vent + Bash/Write allowed, no stacked verdicts.
- **(3) The MCP pin was declarative only** (security): the pinned version+integrity were schema-checked but the spawn ran the descriptor's command as-is — `npx pkg@x` executes whatever the network returns and no pin vouchs for that. Fix: `_spawn_reasons` refuses fetch/spawn-out commands (`npx`/`uvx`/`bunx`/`pipx`/`pip install` incl. `python -m pip`/`curl`/`wget`/`ssh`) and requires the descriptor to reference `{staged}` — the vendored, hash-verified copy inside the staged toolkit; `_apply_toolkit` substitutes the placeholder with the staged root at option build. Enforced in `vet_descriptor`, at load, and at resolve (the one gate, three doors). `test_review3` (red pre-fix): each fetch runner refused by name, a spawn without `{staged}` refused, the vendored form passes, a fetch-runner index entry refuses at load; `test_ac_5`/`_make_mcp` re-scoped to the `{staged}` form (the fixture vendored a real `server.js`).
- **(4) The staged copy was never re-verified** (correctness): resolve verified the source once; the copy was trusted. Fix: `stage_toolkit` verifies the SOURCE before copying (irregular content, per-file hashes vs the index entry — a mutation between resolve and stage refuses) and re-hashes the COPY afterwards against the same entry; any refusal leaves no staged tree. `test_review4` (red pre-fix): a source mutated between resolve and stage refuses naming the file, no partial copy survives; the happy path's staged per-file hashes equal the index's.
- **Also changed:** `tests/test_f22.py` (the four review tests; `test_ac_3` pins the nine-class tuple; `test_ac_5`/`_make_mcp` on the `{staged}` spawn form).
- **Evidence (review round):** 4 review tests red against `1b7e9c8` (plus `test_ac_3`/`test_ac_5` red on the re-scoped shapes); after the fixes 19 F-22 tests / **755 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats), ruff clean. Live proof off-suite pre/post as quoted above (one tiny model turn each; test_f06's live tests never run).

### F-22 owner review round 2 (PR #77; 2 findings — fixed red-first under `test_review5`/`test_review6`)

- **(1) The MCP spawn rule was a bypassable denylist** (security): the round-1 rule refused argv0 ∈ {npx,uvx,bunx,pipx,curl,wget,ssh} plus two exact pip-install spellings and only required `{staged}` in SOME token — the reviewer probed `9769e79` and every one of six shapes was ADMITTED (`python3.12 -m pip install x {staged}/a`; `python3 -I -m pip …` (the `args[:2]` exact match); `npm exec pkg {staged}`; `node -e 'fetch(…npmjs…)' {staged}`; `sh -c 'curl x|sh' {staged}`; `deno run https://x/y.ts {staged}`) — with pypi.org and registry.npmjs.org allowlisted, any of them runs code no index hash vouches for. Replaced with an ALLOWLIST (`_spawn_reasons` rewritten): shape (a) the command itself is a `{staged}/…` vendored executable, or shape (b) the command is an allowed interpreter — node, python, python3, python3.9–3.13, justified as exactly the runtimes a vendored server is realistically written in; each addition is a reviewed change, not a default — whose FIRST non-option argument is a `{staged}/…` script, with only benign runtime flags (`-u -B -E -O -OO -q -qq`, exact tokens) before it, so option bundles (`-Ic`) cannot smuggle a code letter past exact-token matching. Refused: shells and wrappers (sh/bash/zsh/dash/ash/fish/ksh/env — a shell re-parses everything after it), code/module flags anywhere (`-c -e --eval -p --print -m -r --require --import --loader --experimental-loader -I -S`, long `=` forms included), any URL argument, and any `{staged}` token that does not normalise inside the staged root (`{staged}/../x`, bare `{staged}`, placeholder mid-token). Flags match as exact tokens — a flag AFTER the staged script belongs to the script (`--port` is not `-p`). `test_review5` (red: the first probe admitted on `9769e79`): the six probes plus `env sh -c …`, `node --require x {staged}/s.js`, `{staged}/../x` and `python3 -Ic {staged}/server.py` each refused with the probe named in the reason; `node {staged}/server.js`, `python3 {staged}/server.py --port-free-flag`, `{staged}/bin/server --verbose` and `python3 -u {staged}/server.py` admitted. The full probe verdict table (14 shapes) was generated through the real `vet_descriptor` and is in the PR body. Docstring and module-doc wording updated to the allowlist; the earlier round's `test_review3` probes (npx/uvx/… as argv0) all still refuse — now structurally, as "neither a staged executable nor an allowed interpreter".
- **(2) `file_hashes` silently skipped symlinks** (correctness): after round 1, a dir with SKILL.md plus a symlink hashed to just `{SKILL.md: …}` with no error — refusal depended on every caller remembering the separate `irregular_paths` check. `file_hashes` now RAISES `ToolkitError` on any symlink or non-regular file (named), making it the one choke point: `content_hash_of`, `_check_entry`, `stage_toolkit`'s pre- and post-copy verification, and every future caller inherit the refusal. The one deliberate exception is `vet_candidate` — a candidate's link is a *finding* for the report (the `symlink` class), not a crash; it walks via `_scan_tree` and hashes its regular files directly (documented on `file_hashes`). `test_review6` (red: no raise on `9769e79`): `file_hashes` and `content_hash_of` each raise on a symlinked file, a symlinked dir and a fifo.
- **Evidence (round 2):** 2 review tests red against `9769e79`; after the fixes 21 F-22 tests / **757 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend this round, none needed), ruff clean.

### F-22 owner review round 3 (PR #77; 1 finding — fixed red-first under `test_review7`/`test_review8`)

- **(1) Descriptor ENV was a code-injection channel** (security): the round-2 spawn allowlist closed argv, but the descriptor's env still injected code into the MCP server process — the reviewer probed `e17e523` through `_descriptor_reasons` and each of `NODE_OPTIONS="--require /tmp/evil.js"`, `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/evil`, `PYTHONSTARTUP=/tmp/evil.py`, `LD_PRELOAD=/tmp/x.so` was ADMITTED; the child's cwd is the run clone the implementer writes, so relative variants (`--require ./x.js`, `PYTHONPATH=.`) run planted code — exactly what the vendored-only rule exists to stop. Fix, three layers (vet/load/resolve, same as the spawn rule): **env is an allowlist** (`_env_reasons`) — admitted keys LANG, LC_*, TZ, NO_COLOR, TERM, LOG_LEVEL/LOGLEVEL, each justified in the docstring (locale/zone/color/verbosity: inert data, never code, never a path), with per-key value grammars that exclude `$ ~ { } \` and `/` (TZ's slash-separated zone components are dot-free, so no `..` and nothing outside the zone database's fixed root) — **no path can be spelled in a value at all**, which trivially satisfies "no path outside {staged}"; every interpreter/loader control variable refuses with the key named (NODE_OPTIONS/NODE_PATH/NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS, PYTHON* incl. PYTHONPATH/PYTHONSTARTUP/PYTHONHOME/PYTHONUSERBASE/PYTHONINSPECT, LD_PRELOAD/LD_LIBRARY_PATH/LD_AUDIT, DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES/DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH, BASH_ENV, ENV) and so does PATH, HOME and every key outside the set (fail closed). **Session side** (`_apply_toolkit` + `ENV_SCRUB`): the SDK transport was checked — it passes our dict verbatim via `--mcp-config`, and the CLI merges it over its own environment for the child with the config winning on conflicts (the documented `mcpServers.env` behavior; a tokenless boot does not spawn servers, so the merge was verified from the transport code + docs, not a live turn — none allowed this round), so the spawn passes inert neutralizers for every control variable: empty strings where empty means none (verified on this stack: node `execArgv` unchanged with `NODE_OPTIONS=""`; empty `PYTHONHOME`/`PYTHONSTARTUP`/`LD_PRELOAD`/`DYLD_*`/`BASH_ENV` inert), and `/factory-mcp-scrub` for `PYTHONPATH`/`PYTHONUSERBASE` — a path the sandbox's write-allowlist (run dir only) can never let exist, because **empty `PYTHONPATH` is NOT inert**: verified it adds the child's CWD — the implementer-written clone — to `sys.path`, the exact injection this round closes. The config env is exactly scrub + admitted keys, nothing else. `test_review7` (red: the four probes admitted on `e17e523`): 22 refused shapes — the four probes, relative variants, the full control set, PATH/HOME, and paths smuggled into inert keys' values — each refused with the key named, at vet, load and resolve; 6 admitted (LANG/LC_ALL/TZ/NO_COLOR/TERM/LOG_LEVEL). `test_review8` (red: no scrub existed): with NODE_OPTIONS/PYTHONPATH/LD_PRELOAD planted in the parent env, the built child env neutralizes them (`NODE_OPTIONS=""`, `PYTHONPATH=/factory-mcp-scrub`), carries the descriptor's admitted keys, and contains exactly scrub + admitted names. Fixtures re-scoped honestly: the `CACHE_MODE` fixture env was never in the admitted set — `_make_mcp`/`test_ac_5` now use the real admitted keys (NO_COLOR/TZ).
- **Evidence (round 3):** 2 review tests red against `e17e523`; after the fixes 23 F-22 tests / **759 total** passing locally (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend, no live turns this round), ruff clean. The 25-row env probe verdict table (through the real `vet_descriptor`) is in the PR body.
