## F-38 — Parallel review with verifier voting

- **Commits:** `feat/f38-parallel-review` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f38.py` landed first — 13 tests red against the pre-F-38 tree, each on the missing surface it names (`FactoryConfig` has no `reviewers`; `assemble_verifier_prompt` does not import; `run_sessions_parallel` does not import; `review_drops` keys absent from the attempt record and refused by the schema; `assert 6 == 3` — six nits survived, the budget did not exist; `assert 1 == 2` — one reviewer session ran) with the rest of the suite green at that point (13 failed, ruff clean on the new file), then the implementation.
- **Built:** 2026-10-30. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/l2.py` is a protected judge module) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-38 + the queue entry, VALIDATOR-SPEC L2, REVIEW.md, `factory/l2.py`, the reviewer seat in `factory/session.py`, `prompts/reviewer.md`, the L2 wiring in `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/prbody.py`'s validator section, F-23's convergence tests, F-09's reviewer-guard tests). Branched from `origin/main` after rebase onto #75 (pytest-xdist). Anthropic's code-review record (claude.dev blog, Oct 2026; RESEARCH-SOFTWARE-FACTORY.md §15) is the design source: parallel reviewers + a verifier group voting findings in/out; the tags (important / nit / pre-existing) and the "style-only and diagnostic findings get dropped" rule are theirs, mapped onto REVIEW.md's nit budget.
- **Design notes:** the fan-out has three pure stages in `factory/l2.py` and one wiring change in the orchestrator. `merge_reviews` unions N parsed verdicts keyed by the F-23 stable finding key (duplicates two reviewers both report merge into one entry carrying `reported_by`; a duplicate rated more severely keeps the harsher severity; ids regenerate as `U-n` because two reviewers' `R-1` are different findings) and is fail-closed twice: any reviewer's declared `fail` stands, and any invalid verdict fails the whole review exactly as the single invalid one did. `parse_verifier_vote` parses the vote protocol (`{"votes": [{id, keep, reason, tag?}]}`) with the reviewer's own JSON-candidate machinery (fences, last-wins, duplicate keys rejected) and is fail-open only in the keeping direction: an invalid, absent or incomplete vote drops NOTHING (an unverifiable vote may not remove a finding); drops need an explicit vote with a reason, and unknown ids, duplicate votes, missing tags and boolean-shaped `reviewers` config all invalidate. `apply_vote` ranks survivors (severity first, then tag, then union order), forces a `blocking` severity to the `important` tag (a blocking finding tagged "not the author's fault" while it blocks the author's PR is a contradiction — if it predates the diff and should not block, the verifier's tool is the drop, not the re-tag), and mechanically enforces REVIEW.md's nit budget (at most three `minor`/`note` survivors; the rest drop with reason `nit_budget`; `blocking`/`major` never capped). F-23's `apply_convergence` runs unchanged over the post-vote report, with one deliberate extension: its `all_dropped` no-progress case also fires when the verifier voted everything out (a declared fail with nothing left to fix is the same un-actionable round, whatever removed the findings). Verifier and nit-budget drops land in a NEW `verifier_dropped` ledger, deliberately separate from F-23's `dropped`: out-of-delta drops render in the PR body (F-23 finding 9), verifier drops NEVER do (AC-4) — `prbody._validator_section` renders a projection with the key stripped, while the stored validator report and the attempt record keep every entry. The fan-out itself is `session.run_sessions_parallel`: with the real runner it wraps N sessions in ONE integrity guard (scratch symlinks and git baseline once, before any session starts; workspace verified once after all finish — a mutation by any session fails every record of the fan-out, since attribution inside it is impossible and the conservative rule trusts none), gives each session its own srt settings (`<base>-r1.json` …) so concurrent sessions never share a spec file or staged runner, and cleans them all on exit; `cleanup_run` sweeps the `<base>-*` siblings a crash can leave. The verifier seat (`SEATS["verifier"]`) carries the reviewer's full read-only contract — edit tools disallowed, read-only sandbox, the same HEAD/worktree guard — a judge of judges. TraceWriter.append is now under a lock (concurrent session threads forward `tool_call` events through one writer). `prompt_versions` records one hash per fan-out session (`reviewer-1`..`reviewer-N`, one shared assembled prompt, plus `verifier`); `FactoryConfig.reviewers` (config.yaml `reviewers:`, default 2, whole numbers ≥ 1, bools refused) is the knob, and `_sweep_escalation` records the same fan-out shape so F-19 reads one ledger.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f38.py::test_ac_1` — the default config (no file at all) fans out to exactly 2 reviewer sessions with byte-identical prompts over the same diff (asserted equal), a verifier session runs over the union (its prompt carries every finding text and `reported_by`), duplicates merge (`reported_by == [1, 2]`, one entry), survivors are ranked (severity first, then tag) and tagged `important`/`nit`/`pre-existing`, and the declared fail stands. Parallelism itself is pinned two ways: `test_fanout_sessions_run_concurrently` (two stub sessions that must both be in flight before either returns — a barrier; sequential calls time out) and `test_two_real_sandboxes_run_concurrently` (two real srt invocations, concurrent, both confined and returning — the substrate, not the monkeypatched path).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2`, two runs — a passing round whose 6 nit findings (4 minor + 2 note) the verifier keeps: exactly 3 survive (rank order), the 3 capped texts appear nowhere in the PR body, and the attempt record carries 3 `nit_budget` drops; a failing round with blocking + major + 5 nits where the verifier drops one nit as `style-only:` and one as `diagnostic:` before the verdict: both severities survive uncapped, the dropped nits are absent from the findings, and both drop reasons land in `review_drops` keyed by their case.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — the attempt record (through the real queue write, so the schema validates on append) carries `review_drops` with the finding's text/severity/axis and the verifier's reason; the stored validator report carries the same ledger; the drop never reaches the PR body; and the schema owns the field (a drop without a reason is refused on write — the red-first half: before the addition ANY `review_drops` entry was refused, which is how the test ran red).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4`, one case per bullet, scripted seats throughout (no real model anywhere in the file) — (1) two reviewers both report the same defect in different spacing/case: the union has ONE entry (`reported_by == [1, 2]`, the stable F-23 key doing the merging) and the other findings stay separate; (2) the only blocking finding, on a declared pass, is dropped by the verifier with a reason: the verdict flips to pass, the PR opens, and the dropped text appears nowhere in the PR body while the kept survivor does.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — `prompt_versions` has exactly `implementer`, `reviewer-1`, `reviewer-2` (equal hashes: one assembled prompt) and `verifier` (its own file), matching `prompt_versions(profile)`; every review session runs under the task's `max_turns` (20) and the attempt's recorded `turns` are the sum over all four sessions (3+2+2+1 — nothing runs uncounted); the verifier's `timeout_min` is the remaining wall clock after the reviewers and never exceeds theirs; and with a scripted clock burn inside the reviewer sessions the run escalates `budget_exhausted` naming the verifier pass, which never starts (no verifier seat call).
- **Extra coverage:** `test_reviewer_count_comes_from_config` (1 → one session, 3 → three; `prompt_versions` follows); `test_config_reviewers_is_validated` (0, `two`, `2.5`, `true` all refuse; `3` parses); `test_fanout_guard_covers_every_reviewer_session` (the guard spans the fan-out: one mutating session fails BOTH records with `reviewer_mutated_workspace`, restores the tree, tears the scratch down, removes every per-session settings/runner; the shared scratch stays for `cleanup_run`, same as the single-session flow); `test_parse_verifier_vote_shapes` (eight invalid shapes — non-text, non-JSON, no votes list, unknown id, missing tag, bad tag, drop without reason, duplicate votes — each drops nothing and sets the error; an unvoted finding is kept untagged); `test_apply_vote_ranks_and_tags` (severity-then-tag ranking; blocking never tagged below important); `test_verifier_dropped_section_never_reaches_the_pr_body` (the projection, and the input dict is not mutated); `test_no_progress_when_the_verifier_drops_everything` (declared fail + all findings voted out → `all_dropped` no-progress, the escalation path for an un-actionable round).
- **Also changed (all owner-directed in this interactive build):** `factory/l2.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/session.py`, `factory/prompts.py`, `factory/prbody.py`, `factory/trace.py` (protected judge/gate modules) plus `factory/config.py` and `factory/runs.py` (the only non-protected modules touched); `schema/tasks.schema.json` (the `review_drops` attempt field — schema is protected and owner-directed: this PR IS that review); `prompts/verifier.md` (new seat prompt, protected dir); `.factory/project.yaml` (`tests/test_f38.py` under `paths.protected`, the test_f28 lands-with-this-change precedent); mechanical expectation updates only in the protected contract tests `tests/test_f09.py` (prompt_versions keys), `tests/test_f10.py` (seat sequences ×2 reviewers + verifier, prompt_versions set, token counts), `tests/test_f23.py` (round-indexed Scripted stub — a raw reviewer-call count was racy under the concurrent fan-out, which was itself a bug the fan-out exposed — plus one-prompt-per-round helpers), `tests/test_f27.py` (thread-safe stub, first-reviewer vents), `tests/test_f30.py` (session_end rows, 240/60 token totals).
- **Evidence:** 14 F-38 tests (5 AC + 9 focused) / full suite green locally: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **748 passed** in 7:58 (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend; `factory` import-identity confirmed). Red: 13 F-38 tests failed on the pre-implementation tree (see Commits).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` via `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` once this PR's CI is green and you merge it (CI is currently dead on the Actions budget — the local gate is the evidence). No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. F-16 AC-3 (the seeded-bug eval over this reviewer) is the measured follow-up, `manual` by design.

### F-38 owner review round (PR #78; 6 findings — all fixed red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review5`)

Rebased onto `388ed7a` (#76) first: the `factory/orchestrator.py` conflict resolved to keep #76's `attempt_budget_used` (the one budget filter) AND the fan-out's `prompt_versions(profile, reviewers=…)`; `local_binding=` in `_gate_settings` survived the rebase untouched (grep-verified after); #76's implementer-prompt pin `89170d41…` kept — this PR never changes that prompt. Four of the five behavioural tests were red against the pre-fix tree (`test_review1` on the missing `scratch=` parameter, `test_review2` on the missing unanimous override, `test_review3` and the updated `test_ac_5` on `{20} == {8}` — full per-session budgets, not shares — `test_review4` on `first.verdict == "pass"`); `test_review5` (the F-22 seam guard) is a green-on-arrival structural pin in the F-25 review6 style.

- **(1) One shared scratch across the fan-out** (isolation): every fan-out session now gets its OWN `(home, tmp)` — `sandbox_env(scratch=…)` points HOME/TMPDIR/CLAUDE_CODE_TMPDIR/GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR at it, `run_sandboxed(scratch=…)` follows (the command's TMPDIR prefix included), and `build_settings(scratch_write=…)` makes the settings' `allowWrite` EXACTLY that session's scratch: not the run dir's `.home`/`.tmp` (a shared writable dir inside the readable clone is a channel between "independent" reviewers) and not any other session's (each is under the home denyRead, allowWrite-listed only in its owner's settings). ONE git baseline and ONE workspace integrity check still span the fan-out; every per-session artifact (settings, staged runner, scratch) is removed on every exit path, and `cleanup_run`'s `<base>-*` sweep remains the crash backstop. `test_review1_fanout_scratch_is_per_session` (unit: the env shapes; wiring: distinct scratches, exclusive allowWrite, teardown) + `test_review1_real_sandboxes_isolate_their_homes` (two REAL concurrent srt children, each writing then listing its `$HOME`: each sees exactly its own marker).
- **(2) One verifier could undo the fan-out** (correctness): a `blocking`/`major` finding whose `reported_by` covers ALL N reviewers cannot be dropped by the vote — it survives tagged `important`, and the attempted drop is recorded in the ledger as `override_refused: <the verifier's reason>` (the reason kept: a refused drop with no why is an audit hole). Findings fewer than all reviewers reported keep the vote's full semantics. `apply_vote(report, outcome, reviewers=N)`; the orchestrator passes the config's N. `prompts/verifier.md` documents the rule (new hash `d7e5db822401e928ef64d8c032a34b66c3505f0f0fc115ca67fd0d8ea92c694b`), and the round also ADDS the `VERIFIER_FILE` pin to test_f09's `PROMPT_PINS` — the initial commit shipped the file unpinned, itself a review catch. `test_review2_a_unanimous_blocking_finding_survives_the_vote` (unit) + `test_review2_unanimous_blocking_still_fails_the_round` (integration: the round fails, the refused drop is in `review_drops`); `test_ac_4`'s single-reviewer case re-scripted to what it always claimed (one reviewer reporting the blocking finding — with both reviewers scripted identically it had silently become unanimous).
- **(3) Turn budget wasn't aggregate** (correctness): each reviewer session is capped at `(max_turns − turns already spent) // N` (the implementer's actual turns included), the verifier at the remainder after the reviewers' ACTUAL turns; a share below `_REVIEW_TURN_FLOOR` (2) refuses to start — `budget_exhausted` with a note naming the fan-out or the verifier, exactly the wall-clock path's shape. The caps make the sessions' summed turns structurally unable to exceed the task's `max_turns`. `test_review3_turn_budget_is_aggregate`: a passing run's caps and summed turns (15 ≤ 20), refusal before the fan-out (implementer spends 19), refusal before the verifier (reviewers spend down past the floor).
- **(4) Round-one all_dropped escalation** (correctness): a FIRST review whose declared fail had every finding voted out is an unsupported fail — the round passes on L2, the drops stay recorded, and the PR body carries an orchestrator-built sentence in front of the validator JSON ("the reviewers declared FAIL; the verifier voted out every finding, so the effective verdict is pass"). Delta rounds (2+) keep F-23's `all_dropped` no-progress stop exactly as defined (`test_no_progress_when_the_verifier_drops_everything`, now delta-mode). `test_review4_round_one_fail_voted_out_passes` (unit + integration: the PR opens with the sentence).
- **(5) F-22 integration seam** (structure): the fan-out routes every session through `_run_prepared` — the same per-session prepare/run/parse step `run_session_sandboxed` uses — with the option defaults moved INTO `_run_prepared`'s signature (one home), scratch creation in `_scratch_dirs` (one helper), and the fan-out passing only what varies. F-22's rebase must touch: `_run_prepared` (+ `run_session_sandboxed`'s passthrough) for `toolkit=`/staging/`allow_read`; the fan-out's per-session cleanup loop and `cleanup_run`'s sweep for the `-toolkit` siblings. Unknown per-session kwargs already forward unchanged — `test_review5_fanout_forwards_per_session_kwargs` pins it.
- **Also changed:** `factory/sandbox.py` (`sandbox_env`/`run_sandboxed` `scratch=`, `build_settings` `scratch_write=` + the `_allow_write` helper), `factory/session.py` (per-session scratch, `_run_prepared` defaults + seam docstring), `factory/l2.py` (the unanimous rule, the round-one vote-rejected fail), `factory/orchestrator.py` (turn shares + floors, `reviewers=` into `apply_vote`), `factory/prbody.py` (the voted-out sentence), `prompts/verifier.md` (the unanimous rule; re-pinned, pin added), `tests/test_f09.py` (the pin), `tests/test_f38.py` (review tests + the two tests whose premises the findings corrected).
- **Evidence (review round):** 21 F-38 tests / full suite green locally — `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **765 passed** in 5:46 (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend); red per behavioural finding as listed above.

### F-38 on F-22: rebase + the toolkit through the fan-out (PR #78, round 2; owner-directed)

Rebased onto `764c46d` (#77, F-22). Four conflicts, both sides kept: `project.yaml` (F-22's `toolkit/**` / `factory/toolkit.py` / `tests/test_f22.py` AND F-38's `tests/test_f38.py` under `paths.protected`), `BOOTSTRAP.md` (both sections with their evidence, F-22 first — it merged first), `runs.py` (F-22's explicit `-toolkit` rmtree subsumed by F-38's `<base>-*` glob sweep, docstring naming both), `orchestrator.py` (`_sweep_escalation`: F-22's "no session ran: nothing was loaded" skills comment with F-38's reviewers-shaped `prompt_versions`). The item-5 seam from the review round did its job: the rebase auto-merged F-22's `toolkit=` into the fan-out's kwargs; F-22 had grown `toolkit=` on `_run_prepared` (staging at `<settings>-toolkit`, `allow_read` extension, spec carry) exactly where the seam said it would.

- **Toolkit through the fan-out:** every reviewer session gets `toolkit_reviewer` through `run_sessions_parallel(**session_kwargs)` → `_run_prepared(toolkit=…)` — staged per session into its OWN `<settings>-toolkit` dir (hash-verified by `stage_toolkit`, per-session `-r1`/`-r2` spellings), `allow_read` covering exactly that staged dir, the MCP guard hook granted per the resolved items. The **verifier gets the reviewer's narrowed toolkit too** (no "verifier" seat exists in the toolkit config; justification on the call site: the verifier judges the reviewers' findings, so it needs the same read-only evidence surface — giving it none would let reviewers cite evidence the verifier cannot open, and giving it the implementer's wider set would hand a judge write-shaped tools it must never need). The fan-out's per-session cleanup loop removes each `-toolkit` sibling on every exit path; `cleanup_run`'s glob and the F-26 stray regex (rewritten `(-[\w.-]+)?` — every suffix either WO creates, run-id-unique prefix) are the crash backstops. `attempt_record`'s skills union (F-22 AC-8) already collects across the fan-out's records.
- **Tests** (`tests/test_f38.py::test_review6`–`test_review9`): (a) `test_review6` — with the f22 fixture toolkit (skill + `{staged}`-spawned MCP), each fan-out session's spec carries its own distinct staged dir with the vendored files present, the settings allow_read exactly that dir with nothing toolkit-shaped writable, and the built options for BOTH the reviewer and verifier seats carry the plugin dir, the `docs` MCP server and the `mcp__` guard hook (the f22 ac_7 options pattern); (b) `test_review7` — every `-toolkit` (with settings/runner/scratch) gone after the fan-out, on the happy path AND when a session raises mid-fan-out; (c) `test_review8` — end to end through `run_once` with a profile toolkit: all three review sessions receive `("demo-skill", "docs")` and the attempt's `skills` records both items exactly once; (d) `test_review9` — the F-26 stray regex sweeps the base settings, `-runner`/`-scratch`/`-toolkit`, `-r1.json` and its `-runner`/`-toolkit` siblings, `-r1-scratch`, `-v.json-toolkit`, while a foreign project's and a non-run-id file survive.
- **Post-rebase grep:** `attempt_budget_used` (the one budget filter), `local_binding=` in `_gate_settings`, `resolve_toolkit`/`toolkit_reviewer` (per seat), `mcp_guard_hook` (session options) and the unanimous rule (`_unanimous`/`override_refused`) all wired — confirmed before any test ran.
- **Evidence:** F-22 + F-38 suites green together (48 passed) and the full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **794 passed** in 8:01 (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend).
## F-41 — Final full review after fix rounds

- **Commits:** `feat/f41-final-review` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f41.py` landed first — 6 AC tests + 2 merge-defect tests red against the pre-F-41 tree, each on the missing surface it names (module import fails on `final_review_verdict`; `FactoryConfig` has no `final_review_repeats`; `apply_vote` returned **pass** on a declared pass with a surviving major; `apply_convergence` the same; `merge_reviews` kept **2** union entries for the same defect reported in different words at nearby lines of one file; no orchestrator final review, no `final_review` attempt field).
- **Built:** 2026-10-30. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/l2.py` and `factory/orchestrator.py` are protected judge modules) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-41 + the queue entry, VALIDATOR-SPEC L2, REVIEW.md, `factory/l2.py`, the L2 wiring and `_review_prompt`/`_verifier_prompt` in `factory/orchestrator.py`, `prompts/reviewer.md`, `factory/session.py::run_sessions_parallel`, F-23's and F-38's tests, the schema's attempt shape, `factory/config.py`, `factory/trace.py`'s gate_check rows, `factory/prbody.py`'s validator section).
- **Design notes:** the final review is a fourth stage in the round, after the regular L2 pipeline and before publish. When the round's regular review ran in delta mode (an earlier review exists in this run — `last_reviewed` moved) and its verdict passed, ONE more full review runs: the same fan-out shape F-38 built (`run_sessions_parallel`, per-session settings and scratch, the narrowed reviewer toolkit), over the complete `base...head` diff with the round-one prompt shape — `_review_prompt` with no delta and no carried list (AC-1), then the verifier vote. A round whose review was already full (the attempt's first, or the history-rewrite fallback) needs no second one. Convergence runs in full-diff mode (`delta_mode=False`): nothing is out of scope, no `missed_earlier` owed (AC-2), and `final_review_verdict` (new, `factory/l2.py`) applies the final review's own failure rule — only a surviving blocking or major fails it; a declared fail with nothing heavier passes (minors reach the PR body inside the nit budget); a parse error still fails. A failed final review seeds the F-23 state exactly as a failed review round (keys → `prior`, `raised`, `last_reviewed`), its findings become the next implementer round's fix list, and that round's review IS the repeat — `final_pending` skips the delta review (AC-3) — bounded by `FactoryConfig.final_review_repeats` (config.yaml `final_review_repeats:`, whole numbers ≥ 0, bools refused, default 1). Repeats spent → `review_not_converging` before the attempt accounting. Budgets: the final fan-out and its verifier share the attempt's remaining turns with F-38's per-session rule (`remaining // N`, floor 2) and the remaining wall clock; either that cannot fit it escalates `budget_exhausted` — the publish path is unreachable while the trigger holds, so a run never skips the final review to publish in time (AC-4). The peak-window stop applies before its sessions like every session start. The round's delta review stays on the record as `reports["L2-delta"]` once the final owns `L2` (AC-5); `_numbered_findings` and `prbody._validator_section` render its drops like L2's own (verifier drops still never reach the PR body); the attempt record carries `final_review` (verdict, findings, repeat — new schema field) and the trace gets an `L2-final` phase plus the L2 gate_check rows. AC-6: `_fails_round` (blocking or major) now drives the verdict recompute in `apply_vote` AND `apply_convergence` (a declared pass overrides neither; the F-23 stops unchanged, so a repeated major on untouched code still escalates `carried_repeats`) — `REVIEW.md`, `prompts/reviewer.md` (re-pinned `1306ee56…`) and `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` L2 state the rule. One deliberate limit: the repeat budget is per-run state (like F-23's convergence state), not persisted — an interrupted run re-enters with a full-diff review of the fixed code (the same scope a repeat would cover) and the attempt budget stays the cross-run bound.
- **The merge defect (owner-directed extra):** `merge_reviews` keyed duplicates on `finding_key` alone — file+axis+normalised text — so two reviewers describing one bug in different words stayed separate, every heavy finding sat at `reported_by=[1]` and F-38's unanimous rule almost never fired (F-16's run showed exactly this). Duplicates now ALSO merge on location: same file, integer lines within `_MERGE_LINE_WINDOW` (5) of each other, both findings carrying file and line; the text key is tried first, proximity is judged against the first-registered entry's line (no chaining drift), and findings without a line keep the text-key behaviour. Writing the test exposed a latent sibling bug in the same rule: the "keep the harsher severity" comparison was inverted (rank 0 = blocking = harshest, so it fired on the SOFTER duplicate and kept it) — fixed; mixed-severity duplicates now keep the harsher call as the comment always claimed.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f41.py::test_ac_1` — a first-round pass opens the PR with exactly one fan-out (2 reviewer sessions, no final, no `final_review` field); a fix round whose delta review passes runs ONE more full review: 6 reviewer sessions total, the final pair byte-identical over the complete diff with `## Diff (base...head)` and NO delta section and NO carried-findings section, the delta review's prompt distinct, the verifier vote after it, PR only after the final passes.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2`, two runs — (1) the final review reports a major on `other.py`, a file the fix round never touched, with no `missed_earlier`: it stands (`dropped == []`, severity major, `missed_earlier` absent), fails the round, nothing publishes; (2) a declared FAIL with five minor findings: the PR opens, nits 1–3 reach the body within the nit budget, nits 4–5 never appear, and the attempt record carries the 2 `nit_budget` drops.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — the knob (`FactoryConfig().final_review_repeats == 1`; `2`/`0` parse; `-1` and `true` refuse); a failed final review sends its findings to the next implementer round (asserted in round 3's fix prompt, after round 2's carried round-1 findings), the repeat round runs L0/L1 (stored reports pass) and a repeat of the final review over the full diff; the repeat failing again escalates `review_not_converging` (`[fail, fail, escalated]`, nothing published); `final_review_repeats: 0` escalates on the first failure (`[fail, escalated]`).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4`, both budgets — turn: the delta round leaves 3 turns (max 10), the final fan-out cannot fit 2 sessions above the floor → `budget_exhausted`, the note names the final review, 4 reviewer sessions total (no final fan-out), no PR; wall-clock: a scripted burn inside the delta reviewers leaves no clock → `budget_exhausted` before the final, same shape. The PR never opens in either case: the publish path is unreachable while the final is owed.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5`, the end-to-end gap — round 1 ships `other.py`'s untested helper; round 2's fix to `impl.py` breaks it while never touching the file; the delta review passes clean (its stored findings are empty); the final review catches the major on `other.py` with no `missed_earlier`, no PR opens; round 3 fixes it and the repeat passes → PR. Attempt records: `[fail, fail, pass]` with `final_review` absent on attempt 1, `fail`/`repeat: false` (findings `[other.py, major]`) on attempt 2, `pass`/`repeat: true` on attempt 3; the stored report keeps both reviews of the failing round (`L2-delta` pass, `L2` fail); the trace carries two `L2-final` phases and the finding's gate_check rows.
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — unit: `apply_vote` and `apply_convergence` each fail a declared pass with a surviving major (delta and full-diff rounds both covered); a repeated major on an untouched file is a carried bounce (`no_progress`, `carried_repeats`) so the F-23 stop escalates instead of looping; `final_review_verdict` flips a declared fail with only minors to pass. Orchestrator: a round-1 declared pass with a surviving major never opens a PR (needs_human, no PR, stored L2 verdict fail). `REVIEW.md` carries the exact rule sentence and the assembled reviewer prompt states it (pin `1306ee568d0f09b02aef2c34694d61e343163a1abde54d19001fba9f55acba22` in `tests/test_f09.py`).
- **Merge-defect tests:** `test_merge_reviews_merges_on_file_and_nearby_line` (different words, lines 10/13 of one file → ONE union entry, `reported_by == [1, 2]`; a harsher duplicate keeps `blocking`; the unanimous rule fires — a verifier drop is refused with `override_refused`, the finding survives tagged `important` and fails the round) and `test_merge_reviews_keeps_the_text_key_otherwise` (same text without location still merges; different words with no line stay separate; lines 4 vs 400 on one file stay separate).
- **Also changed (all owner-directed in this interactive build):** `factory/l2.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/config.py`, `factory/prbody.py` (protected judge/gate modules); `schema/tasks.schema.json` (the `final_review` attempt field — protected, owner-directed: this PR IS that review); `prompts/reviewer.md` (protected dir; re-pinned); `REVIEW.md`, `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` (the verdict rule stated where the old text said blocking-only); `.factory/project.yaml` (`tests/test_f41.py` under `paths.protected`, the F-38 precedent); mechanical expectation updates only in the protected contract tests `tests/test_f23.py` (the `Scripted` stub gained a `final` script for the round's second fan-out — default clean pass — plus round tracking the three amender-style wrappers now maintain; `test_ac_2`/`review7`/`review9` re-anchored to the `L2-delta` record and the extra final fan-out) and `tests/test_f09.py` (the reviewer pin).
- **Real-session proof** (scripted seats have missed real crashes twice): ONE production run from the real `~/factory` root — real config (`reviewers: 2` default, `final_review_repeats: 1` default, peak window disabled by owner), the real keychain `factory-zai` credential against the Z.ai endpoint, the real sdk probe, a real implementer session, real L0/L1 through the pinned srt sandbox, the real F-38 fan-out through `run_sessions_parallel` (per-session settings and scratch) and the real verifier vote — `run_once` with only the three GitHub seams stubbed (`open_pr`, branch protection, `pr_state`; the fixture repo has no GitHub). Tiny prompts by construction (one-function repo, one-line diff). Statuses: **pr_opened** (`run_end: published`), all four sessions `completed` — implementer + 2 reviewers + verifier, 13 turns, 82,551 input / 3,033 output tokens; the reviewers reported one genuine minor (the implementer committed `__pycache__` bytecode), the union merged it to one entry, the verifier kept it, the verdict passed and the stubbed PR opened — the whole L2 pipeline for real. No final review ran because the task passed on round one (AC-1's skip rule); the final review rides this same fan-out + verifier production path, and a first probe with a 12-turn budget deliberately ended `budget_exhausted` at the fan-out's turn floor (10 implementer turns left 2 < 2×floor) — AC-4's refusal firing on the real path too. Trace kept at `/tmp/f41-real-trace.jsonl`; every `f41real` artifact was removed from `~/factory` after the run (the real root left as found).
- **Evidence:** 8 F-41 tests (6 AC + 2 merge-defect) / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **829 passed** in 5:36 (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; stub seats — no model spend in the suite; the real spend is the proof run above). Red: the six AC tests failed on the pre-implementation tree on the surfaces listed under Commits.
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` via `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` once this PR's gate is green and you merge it (CI is dead on the Actions budget — the local gate is the evidence). No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. F-42 is next in the queue.

### F-41 owner review (PR #88): location merge could erase a defect

Owner probe: two different majors three lines apart ("SQL injection", "Off-by-one") merged into one finding reported by both reviewers, so the second defect left the verdict and the unanimous rule protected a finding nobody corroborated. A location merge now also needs overlapping wording (`_shares_wording`), and any location merge keeps every reporter's text. Red-first: `test_merge_reviews_keeps_different_bugs_apart_at_nearby_lines`, `test_a_location_merge_keeps_every_reporters_wording`.

## F-42 — Session hardening: attribution and sandbox-bypass attempts

- **Commits:** `feat/f42-session-hardening` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f42.py` landed first and failed on the missing surfaces it names — collection died on `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'factory.attribution'` (the one pattern list, AC-1/AC-2), and behind it `run_l0` produced no `attribution_trailer` finding, `gh_pr_create` opened PRs carrying trailer titles, `build_options` carried no attribution settings and no sandbox-bypass hook, `_tap_tool_calls` took no denial state, and `SessionRecord` had no `sandbox_bypass_denials`.
- **Built:** 2026-10-31. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/session.py`, `factory/l0.py`, `factory/publish.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/trace.py`, `schema/**`, `prompts/**` are protected) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-42 + the queue entry, `factory/l0.py`, `factory/publish.py`, `factory/prbody.py`, the hook/options/seat sections of `factory/session.py`, `factory/status.py`, `factory/trace.py`, the orchestrator's round loop and attempt record, the pinned claude-agent-sdk 0.2.168's `ClaudeAgentOptions`/`--settings` plumbing, the bundled CLI binary's own settings schema and attribution resolver, `tests/test_f09.py`'s prompt pins, `.factory/project.yaml`'s protected list, this ledger's parser format in `factory/approve.py`).
- **Design notes:** one pattern list (`factory/attribution.py`, itself protected) covers the AI classes AC-1 names — a `Co-Authored-By` naming an AI author (assistant names and the factory's own seat identities, the #58/#61 shape) or an AI vendor address (`@anthropic.com` etc.; `copilot@github.com` named exactly so GitHub's human `@users.noreply.github.com` addresses pass), and `Generated with` footers — matched on the commit messages between the merge-base and head on every L0 round (`attribution_trailer` FAIL, `factory/l0.py::attribution_findings`; the trace's L0 gate_checks gain a `commit_messages` row, said even when clean), and refused by `gh_pr_create` on the PR title and body before gh runs (AC-2), so a squash commit — which builds its message from that title and body — cannot inherit a trailer through either path. A human `Co-Authored-By` passes everywhere. AC-3 uses the pinned CLI's real switch, found in the bundled binary's own schema (not guessed): the deprecated boolean is `includeCoAuthoredBy` ("Use attribution instead"), the modern form is the `attribution` object whose resolver returns "disabled" on `commitTrailers: false` — so every seat's options now carry `settings={"attribution": {"commitTrailers": false, "commit": "", "pr": ""}}` (the schema's own words: "Empty string hides attribution"), delivered through the SDK's `--settings`, which applies with `setting_sources=[]` (that flag only shuts filesystem discovery off). `prompts/implementer.md` states both rules (no attribution, never the bypass flag) and the pin is re-pinned (`4855e4ba…` in `tests/test_f09.py`). AC-4 is a third PreToolUse hook (`sandbox_bypass_hook`, matcher Bash) in `build_options`, so every seat carries it — implementer, the F-38 fan-out reviewers and the verifier all build their options there — denying any Bash call whose input sets `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` (truthy-match, the deny-layer direction) with the reason "the sandbox is not negotiable"; a deny hook still blocks under `bypassPermissions` (F-06's established fact), which is the mode every seat runs in. AC-5: the hook counts into a `BypassDenials` state shared with the F-30 tap (the denied call's `tool_call` event is stamped `denied: "sandbox_bypass"`, closed or flushed open at stream end — only `tool_call` events cross the sandbox pipe, so the mark rides inside the allowed type) and with `SessionRecord.sandbox_bypass_denials`, which the orchestrator sums onto the attempt record (new schema field) and `TraceWriter.session_end` renders; `factory status` shows the week's total in its own section off the same week window as the quota.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f42.py::test_ac_1` — a branch commit with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` FAILs L0 with `attribution_trailer` naming the commit sha and the full matched line; the other real shapes each FAIL (`Claude Code <noreply@anthropic.com>`, `Copilot <copilot@github.com>`, `Software Factory <factory@localhost>`, the `🤖 Generated with [Claude Code]` footer); a human co-author passes (`Jane Reviewer <jane@example.com>` and main's own `AgenticTony <agentictony@users.noreply.github.com>` shape); a trailer BELOW the merge-base (on main itself, where five already sit) never fails the branch; and the match comes from the same `attribution_match` publish uses.
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — the real `gh` seam with a fake gh binary: a clean title/body opens the PR (gh really ran); a title carrying the footer and a body carrying the Claude trailer are each refused `PushRefused` naming the match, with gh never invoked (its argv log stays empty); a human co-author in the body is not a refusal.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3`, parametrized over every seat — `build_options` yields `settings` with `attribution == {"commitTrailers": false, "commit": "", "pr": ""}` and `setting_sources == []` unchanged; the assembled implementer prompt states the rule; the pin is updated (`4855e4ba…`, `tests/test_f09.py`). Real-session half below.

### F-42 owner review (PR #89): pattern precision and the push ordering

Four findings, all fixed on the same branch (`fix: attribution patterns and pre-push PR check (F-42)`), red-first under `test_review1`–`test_review4`.

1. **`generated with` matched the phrase anywhere.** The probed ordinary lines ("docs: client SDK generated with openapi-generator", "Fixture data generated with faker", "report generated with ruff") failed L0 on legitimate text. The footer pattern now requires the phrase followed — within two connecting tokens, so "Generated with GitHub Copilot" reaches its tool — by an AI tool (bracketed or bare: `[Claude Code]`, Claude, Copilot, Cursor, Codex, Gemini, ChatGPT, GPT, OpenAI, Anthropic) or an AI vendor link, anywhere in a line (a title carries it mid-line too). The ordinary-prose lines name no AI tool and pass; the real footer lines still fail.
2. **The PR-text refusal fired after the push.** The check lived inside `gh_pr_create`, downstream of `push_branch`, so a refusal stranded a remote branch under `pr_create_failed` — and the body's fenced sections (implementer notes, the validator JSON) legitimately QUOTE trailers when reviewers discuss attribution. New `factory.publish.check_pr_text(title, body)`: the title scans unanchored (a title is one line, so a trailer anywhere in it is deliberate — `T-1: add Co-Authored-By: Claude support` refuses), the body scans free text only (fences stripped, an unclosed fence stays scanned — fail closed); the orchestrator calls it BEFORE `push_branch` (a refusal is the deterministic `publish_refused` class, no orphan branch), and `gh_pr_create` keeps the same rule as its backstop. Squash inheritance stays closed: the squash message comes from the title (checked) and the branch commits (L0-checked).
3. **Bare ambiguous names flagged humans.** `Co-authored-by: Kimi Larsson <kimi@acme.se>` failed — a real colleague credited illegitimately. The name list is split: unambiguous product names (Claude, Anthropic, Copilot, ChatGPT, GPT-n, Codex, OpenAI, the factory's own seat names) match alone; names that are also human names (Kimi, Gemini, Mistral, Llama, Cursor, Qwen, GLM, Moonshot) flag only with an AI shape beside them — a bot/noreply address, an AI vendor address (moonshot.ai, deepseek.com, mistral.ai, claude.com/ai joined the vendor domains), or the product's compound name ("Cursor Agent", "Gemini Code Assist"). A human Kimi at their company address passes; `Kimi <bot@moonshot.ai>` fails.
4. **Other trailer styles slipped through.** `Assisted-by:`, `Generated-by:` and `AI-assisted:` now follow the same rules as `Co-Authored-By` (one shared trailer-prefix alternation, so a rule change applies to all four), and the bare `AI-assisted: yes` declaration fails on its own — that trailer exists only to declare AI assistance.

Patterns were restructured once, not patched four times: one core list (`_PATTERN_CORES`) compiled anchored (line-initial trailers/declarations for commit messages and bodies, where prose may discuss a trailer) and unanchored (titles) — never two hand-maintained lists. Two implementation defects the red tests caught mid-round: the anchored prefix class originally absorbed the newline itself (empty matched lines; now `[^\w\n]{0,8}`, which also correctly absorbs the footer's emoji), and the title needed the unanchored form rather than a widened prefix (a widened prefix would have unanchored commit messages too, tripping prose that discusses trailers). `VALIDATOR-SPEC.md` L0 states the widened rule. Red on the pre-fix tree (`bb87c84`): all four review tests failed on the behaviour their finding names — the probe lines hit (or passed) the wrong way through `attribution_match`, and review2 failed on the missing `check_pr_text` surface with the branch already pushed. Green: 13 f42 tests; full gate **843 passed**, ruff clean.

Second review round (PR #89): `tests/test_f42.py::test_review5_mid_line_footer_and_suffixed_model_names` red on `dd4ef9d` — a mid-line "generated with Claude Code" in a commit body passed L0, `aider (gpt-4o)` and `cursoragent@cursor.com` co-authors passed. Fix: the footer core matches unanchored in every surface (its AI-tool requirement keeps prose out), `gpt-?\d\w*` covers suffixed model names, `cursor.com` joins the vendor domains. Green: 14 f42 tests; full gate **844 passed**, ruff clean.

## F-43 — Egress redaction seam

- **Commits:** `feat/f43-egress-redaction` → PR (this one). Red-first: `tests/test_f43.py` landed first and failed on the gaps each AC names — AC-1 on the literal seat token reaching the rendered PR body through the real `run_once` publish path (the C-1 hole itself), AC-2/AC-3 on `TypeError` (`check_pr_text`/`flush_vents` took no `secrets`), AC-4 on `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'factory.egress'`, AC-5 on the JWT shape passing `redact_credentials` unredacted, AC-6 on "orchestrator.py:2772: implementer notes bypass the seam".
- **Built:** 2026-11-05. Interactive session (owner-directed: `factory/prbody.py`'s input path, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/orchestrator.py`, `factory/session.py`, `factory/trace.py` are protected; `factory/publish.py`, `factory/vent.py`, `factory/status.py` rode the same owner direction — the seam touches them all) after the full re-read (CLAUDE.md, BUILD-ORDER §F-43, CODE-REVIEW-2026-11-05 C-1/M-8/m-16/I-10, docs/remediation-plan-2026-11-05 §F-43, `factory/trace.py`, `factory/sentinels.py`, `factory/prbody.py`, `factory/publish.py`, `factory/vent.py`, `factory/status.py`, the orchestrator's PR-body path and trace setup, `factory/session.py`'s stdout-dump path, `tests/test_f33.py`, the test_f42 `_Env`/`_Seats` harness, `.factory/project.yaml`).
- **Design notes:** ONE seam, `factory/egress.py::scrub_session_text(text, secrets=())` — literal scrub (`scrub_literal`, one per caller-known secret), then `redact_credentials`, then `strip_control_sequences`, in exactly that order (literals first so no later rewrite can leave a fragment behind; the strip last so redaction judged the text as written). The signature takes a SEQUENCE of secrets, never "the seat token": F-59's per-provider credentials thread through the same parameter. `scrub_literal`/`MIN_SECRET_LEN`/`REDACTED` moved from `factory/trace.py` to `factory/sentinels.py` (beside `redact_credentials`, so the seam composes one home); `factory.trace` re-exports them unchanged — one implementation, and `factory/session.py`'s existing import keeps working. `scrub_session_value` is the recursive form for report dicts (keys included — a hostile finding dict can carry a secret in a key the body renders); it scrubs a projection and never mutates the stored reports. Scrub-then-cap everywhere the PR body cuts (F-27's invariant): the implementer notes are seam-scrubbed whole, THEN capped at `_PR_NOTES_CAP`; the reports are seam-scrubbed, then `_compact` cuts the tails. `check_pr_text` is deliberately NOT the seam: a second, independent fence (AC-2) that scans title AND body — fences included — for every caller secret and refuses, naming the refusal without echoing the token; it assumes the seam may have failed, so it never depends on it having run. Vents are seam-scrubbed TWICE (the F-27 invariant): in-sandbox at `VentCollector.record` (the default collector now carries the session's token) and again at `flush_vents` (the orchestrator hands `egress_secrets`); the unparsable-record dump (`_save_session_stdout`) takes the session token and writes seam-scrubbed text. Stripping is at PRINT, not at write (AC-4's contract line): the stored trace/progress log keep their content — the seam's control-strip applies to what a terminal executes: `read_trace` (also the dashboard's SSE/run page follower) and `render_status` (CLI print and dashboard page) strip at their one reading/render seam; vents are the deliberate exception — the contract says vent text passes the full seam, so stored vents are stripped at the flush (the collector already stripped in-sandbox; the flush is idempotent). `egress_secrets = (seat_token() or "",)` is computed once per run beside the `TraceWriter` construction and feeds both, so no channel scrubs a different secret set.
- **AC-1** proven: `tests/test_f43.py::test_ac_1` — the real `run_once` pipeline (clone manager, queue, L0/L1 through the pinned srt sandbox, real `build_pr_body`, real `check_pr_text`, real `push_branch` against a bare remote; only the seats scripted, `orchestrator.seat_token` planted) publishes with the literal token AND a bare `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<jwt>` echo planted in the implementer's result and in a passing review's one minor finding: neither reaches the rendered body, the finding still renders with `[redacted]` (the path was exercised, not skipped), and the echo's value is pattern-redacted (`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=[redacted]`).
- **AC-2** proven: `test_ac_2` — `check_pr_text` with the RAW (never seam-scrubbed) body refuses `PushRefused` naming "literal session credential" without echoing the token or its value; the title refuses too; clean text passes; a literal below `MIN_SECRET_LEN` is noise, not a refusal.
- **AC-3** proven: `test_ac_3` — `flush_vents` with `secrets` writes the task's progress log with neither the token, nor the echoed JWT, nor any `\x1b` byte on disk; the in-sandbox `VentCollector(secrets=...)` records the same scrub before anything crosses the boundary; `_save_session_stdout(..., secrets=...)` writes the dump without token/echo/control bytes while keeping the ordinary text ("rewritten" survives — the seam shreds commands, not content).
- **AC-4** proven: `test_ac_4` — `strip_control_sequences` removes the three m-16 shapes (screen clear `\x1b[2J\x1b[H`, OSC-8 hyperlink with ST terminator, cursor move `\x1b[5A\x1b[?25l`) keeping surrounding text and `\t`/`\n`; `read_trace` over a trace file carrying an OSC-8 link prints no escape byte; `render_status` over a vent logged through the seam renders the link's text with no `\x1b` anywhere on the page.
- **AC-5** proven: `test_ac_5` — for each new shape (`JSON web token`, `bearer token`, `unquoted YAML password`): `redact_credentials` removes the value, the `credential_added` sentinel fires on an added line naming the shape without echoing the value, and a fresh F-33 attack diff (`corpus/attack/credential_added-{7,8,9}.patch`) flags through the real replay under this repo's real risk globs. The benign corpus false-positive count: **0 before, 0 after** (measured 2026-11-05 by replaying all 41 benign patches through `patch_findings` against `.factory/project.yaml`'s globs; `tests/test_f33.py` 25/25 green both sides).
- **AC-6** proven: `test_ac_6` — the channel list derived by grepping the writers, pinned structurally: (1) every `build_pr_body` call scrubs BOTH session-text inputs (`implementer_notes` via `scrub_session_text`, `reports` via `scrub_session_value`); (2) a `check_pr_text` call passes `secrets`; (3) every `flush_vents` call passes `secrets`; (4) `TraceWriter` takes `extra_secrets`; (5) `session.py`'s `_save_session_stdout` call passes the token. The tripwire: any call in `factory/orchestrator.py` receiving session-derived text (`.result`/`.reason`/`.vents`/`.findings`) whose callee is not the seam, a guarded writer, a local-disk sink (attempt record/queue/progress-log findings/stored reports/trace writer), a prompt builder (ingress into the next session — the review's own function), or a pure expression op, fails naming file:line — a NEW channel cannot silently skip the seam. Detection verified by mutation: planted `print(impl.result)`, `gh_pr_comment(union.findings)` and `print(record.vents)` each trip it (checked against the AST logic, 2026-11-05). The terminal/off-machine reading seams (`read_trace`, `render_status`) are pinned by AC-4's behaviour tests plus the dashboard's own contract that both go through them.
- **Hostile pass (pre-commit):** planted-token probes against every channel. **One real leak found and fixed red-first**: a token SPLIT BY A CONTROL SEQUENCE (`<half>\x1b[0m<half>`, and the same inside the env echo's JWT) survived the literal scrub — not the literal while split — and the strip then MERGED the halves into the whole token after every scrub had run. Fix: the strip is itself a text-rewriting boundary, so when it changes the text the scrubbers re-judge the rewritten form (no-op cost when nothing was stripped); the contract's composition order stands, `test_hostile_control_split_secret_does_not_reassemble` pins it. Blocked as designed: the raw literal in notes/finding/vent/dump, the bare `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=<jwt>` echo, quoted, `Bearer <token>`, unquoted YAML `password:`, a control-split inside an L2 finding, a token in a finding KEY / parse_error / dropped / verifier-dropped text (the recursive scrub covers keys), and a token inside a fenced PR-body block (the seam scrubs the input BEFORE `prbody` builds the fence, and `check_pr_text` scans fences for literals — probed live). Known limits, unchanged from pre-F-43 and out of this contract: a token split across newlines (the seam keeps line structure; the trace writer has had the same limit since F-30) and an encoded token (base64 — no shape list decodes; L0's diff scan and F-59's provider work own that class).
- **Also changed (owner-directed in this interactive build):** protected judge/gate modules `factory/sentinels.py` (three credential shapes; `scrub_literal`/`MIN_SECRET_LEN`/`REDACTED` moved in, re-exported from `factory/trace.py`), `factory/trace.py` (the re-export; `read_trace` strips at print), `factory/orchestrator.py` (`egress_secrets`; seam at the PR-body build; `secrets` to the fence and every vent flush), `factory/session.py` (collector carries the token; the dump takes `secrets`), `factory/publish.py` (`check_pr_text` literal fence), `factory/prbody.py` UNTOUCHED (the seam wraps its inputs at the orchestrator call site — the builder stays pure). Not protected, same direction: `factory/vent.py` (seam at record and flush), `factory/status.py` (strip at render). New: `factory/egress.py`, `tests/test_f43.py`, `corpus/attack/credential_added-{7,8,9}.patch`. `.factory/project.yaml` gains `tests/test_f43.py` under `paths.protected` (the PR #93/test_f16 class of gap, closed in the same PR). `.factory/tasks.json` untouched — no approve, no reconcile (owner's, after merge). Open recommendation for the owner: `factory/egress.py` itself is not yet a `paths.protected` entry — behaviorally guarded by the protected `tests/test_f43.py`, but the F-42 `factory/attribution.py` precedent suggests listing it; left out here because the owner's instruction named only the test file.
- **Evidence:** 7 F-43 tests (6 ACs + the hostile-pass finding test) / F-33 corpus gate 25/25 (3 new attack diffs covered by its glob) / full gate: `ruff check .` clean; `PYTHONPATH=. .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -n auto --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_3 --deselect tests/test_f06.py::test_ac_4` → **977 passed** (macOS, real srt where the suites sandbox; scripted seats — no model spend, no real sessions this WO).
- **Status to set at reconcile:** `done` via `factory approve --reconcile-bootstrap` once this PR's gate is green and you merge it. No deploy target, so L4 doesn't apply. Next Tier 1 WO: F-44.
